## Deviations from study plan

Participant enrolment into phase 3 started under protocol version 7.0 (dated 7 February 2022). The protocol was subsequently amended 3 times. The most important changes are summarised below.

## Protocol version 8 (dated 05 April 2022)

1047 participants had been recruited at the time of this amendment.

- Primary endpoint was changed from 'time to sustained improvement' to 'time to sustained resolution' of COVID-19 symptoms.
- Primary analysis population was changed from including 'all randomised participants with confirmed positive RT-PCR result' to 'all randomised participants with SARS-CoV-2 viral titer detected at baseline.'
- Target sample size was increased from 420 to 595 participants per group.
- The interim efficacy analysis was deleted.

## Version 9 (dated 08 July 2022)

1810 participants had been recruited at the time of the amendment.

- Primary analysis population was changed back from 'all randomised participants with SARSCoV-2 viral titer' to 'all randomised participants with confirmed positive RT-PCR result.'
- Target samples size was decreased from 595 to 530 participants per group.
- The secondary endpoint of 'change from baseline on Day 4 in the amount of SARS-CoV-2 viral RNA' was changed into a key secondary endpoint. It was also added to the fixed testing sequence.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## Version 10 (dated 20 September 2022)

Participant recruitment had been completed at the time of the amendment.

- The number of symptoms included in the primary endpoint was changed from 12 to 5.
- The efficacy analyses were restricted to participants with &lt;72 hours from the onset of COVID19 to randomisation.
- The comparison of the high dose (250 mg) to placebo was changed from being a confirmatory analysis to a descriptive analysis.
- The multiple-testing strategy was changed so that instead of testing each S-217622 dose against placebo at an alpha of 2.5% (two-sided), only the 125 mg dose would be tested at an alpha of 5% (two-sided).
- The primary statistical analysis was changed from a stratified log-rank test to a Peto-Prentice stratified generalized Wilcoxon test.

Database lock: 22 September 2022.

Unblinding: 23 September 2022.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## Outcomes and estimation

## Primary Efficacy Endpoint

The primary efficacy endpoint was the time to sustained (≥ 24 hours) resolution of 5 COVID-19 symptoms (see table footnote) in participants with mild/moderate SARS-CoV-2 infection.

## Ancillary analyses

## Hospitalisation and death

The proportion of participants reaching each score on 8-Point ordinal scale in Phase 3 part in the ITT population with less than 72 hours from COVID-19 onset to randomization was investigated.

At baseline the proportion of participants with score ≥ 3 i.e., hospitalised without oxygen therapy, was 0.6% (2/339), 0.9% (3/333) and 0% in the 125 mg, 250 mg and placebo groups respectively. There were no participants with higher score at baseline.

At Day 2 the proportion of participants with score ≥ 3 was 0.3% (1/330) in the 250 mg group and none in the 125 mg or placebo groups were hospitalised. There were no participants with a higher score at Day 2.

During treatment /follow-up period 0.6% (2/330) of participants in the 250 mg group had a score ≥ 3.

## Time to resolution of 12 COVID-19 symptoms

Before protocol version 10.0, the primary efficacy analysis in SCORPIO-SR Phase 3 Part was the time to resolution of 12 COVID-19 symptoms (see definition in table footnote) in the intention-to-treat population, which included participants that were randomised regardless of time from onset of COVID19 symptoms.

## Viral rebound

Viral RNA rebound rates were calculated based on either a ≥ 0.5 log10 RNA copies/mL increase (FDA definition) or a ≥ 1.0 log10 RNA copies/mL increase (Applicants definition) following the peak viral load (full definitions are described in the footnotes of Tables below).

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## Symptomatic rebound

One participant experienced symptomatic rebound in the ensitrelvir 375/125 mg group in the ITT population who were randomised within 72 hours in the SCORPIO-SR Phase 3 part. However, viral RNA rebound was not observed in that one symptomatic rebound case.

## 5.3.2.4. SCORPIO-HR (2117T1224, ACTIV-2d/A5407)

## 5.3.2.4.1. Study title

A Phase 3, multicenter, randomized, double-blind, 24-week study of the clinical and antiviral effect of S-217622 compared with placebo in non-hospitalized participants with COVID-19 (SCORPIO-HR study)

## 5.3.2.4.2. Study design

This study was a Phase 3, multicentre, randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled study that evaluated the safety and efficacy of ensitrelvir for the treatment of symptomatic SARS-CoV-2 infection in high-risk and standard-risk nonhospitalised adults.

The study consisted of a Screening visit; a Study Entry/Randomization visit (Day 1); and visits on Days 4, 8, 16, and 29, and Weeks 12 and 24. Additionally, there was an event driven evaluation visit for worsening symptoms between Days 6 to 29, premature discontinuation visits for discontinuations before Day 29 and after Day 29, and an event driven evaluation visit for SARS-CoV-2 reinfection, as applicable.

The study was initiated on 03 August 2022 and was completed on 02 March 2024 (last participant last visit).

## 5.3.2.4.3. Study population

Key inclusion criteria (All Participants)

- Age ≥ 18 years
- Documentation of laboratory-confirmed active SARS-CoV-2 infection, as determined by a nucleic acid (eg, RT-PCR) or antigen test from any respiratory tract specimen (eg, oropharyngeal, nasopharyngeal or nasal swab, or saliva) collected ≤ 72 hours (3 days) prior to randomization
- Participants were expected to begin study intervention ≤ 3 days (or ≤ 5 days under protocol versions 1.0 to 3.0) from self-reported date of onset of any of the COVID-19-related symptoms from the following list: Cough; Shortness of breath or difficulty breathing; Feeling feverish; Chills; Fatigue; Body pain or muscle pain or aches; Diarrhea; Nausea; Vomiting; Headache; Sore throat; Nasal obstruction or congestion; Nasal discharge; Loss of taste; Loss of smell
- One or more of the signs/symptoms, described above, present within 24 hours prior to randomization (all criteria above except loss of taste or loss of smell):
- Oxygen saturation of ≥ 92% on room air adjusted for altitude and obtained at rest by study staff within 24 hours prior to randomization; for a potential participant who regularly received

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

chronic supplementary oxygen for an underlying lung condition, oxygen saturation measured while on their standard home oxygen supplementation level was to be ≥ 92%

## For High-risk Participants (Excluded in the United States)

Participants at increased risk of progression to severe COVID-19 were defined as follows:

- Age ≥ 65 years
- Age ≥ 18 with 1 of the following:
- o Obesity (body mass index [BMI] ≥ 30 kg/m 2 ); BMI was rounded to the nearest whole number, for example 29.5 kg/m 2 was rounded to 30 kg/m 2
- o Diabetes mellitus
- o Hypertension requiring daily prescribed therapy
- o Cardiovascular disease requiring daily prescribed therapy or congenital heart disease
- o Chronic lung disease (eg, chronic obstructive pulmonary disease, moderate to severe asthma, interstitial lung disease, cystic fibrosis, pulmonary hypertension) requiring daily prescribed therapy
- o Chronic kidney disease, defined as known current kidney impairment with a CrCl or estimated glomerular filtration rate &lt; 60 mL/min/1.73m 2 within the past 12 months prior to randomization, as long as the participant does not have known CrCl &lt; 30 mL/min by Cockcroft-Gault or require dialysis
- o Down syndrome
- o Sickle cell disease
- o One of the following immunocompromising conditions or immunosuppressive treatments:
-  Receiving chemotherapy or other therapies for cancer
-  Hematologic malignancy (active or in remission)
-  History of a hematopoietic stem cell or a solid organ transplant
-  HIV infection: not on antiretroviral therapy or with CD4+ cell count &lt; 200 cells/mm 3
-  Combined primary immunodeficiency disorder
-  Taking immunosuppressive medications (eg, drugs to suppress rejection of transplanted organs or to treat rheumatologic and gastrointestinal conditions, such as antitumor necrosis factor agents, mycophenolate, and rituximab); current use of some corticosteroids was exclusionary due to concern for possible drug-drug interaction with ensitrelvir

## For Standard-risk Participants (All Countries)

Participants at standard risk for progression to severe COVID-19 were defined as ≥ 18 to &lt; 65 years of age and meeting all inclusion criteria for all participants, with none of the risk factors in the inclusion criteria for high-risk participants.

## Key exclusion criteria

- History of hospitalization for the current SARS-CoV-2 infection (ie, prior hospitalization for a prior episode of SARS-CoV-2 infection was allowable)
- For the current SARS-CoV-2 infection, any positive SARS-CoV-2 molecular (nucleic acid) or antigen test from any respiratory tract specimen (e.g., oropharyngeal, nasopharyngeal or nasal swab, or saliva) collected ˃ 72 hours (3 days) prior to randomization. Participants with reinfection, defined as prior SARS-CoV-2 infection that began &gt; 90 days prior to the current onset of symptoms with interval resolution of symptoms, were eligible as long as the current infection had not been present for more than 3 days prior to randomization

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

- Current need for hospitalisation or immediate medical attention in the opinion of the investigator
- Known allergy/sensitivity or any hypersensitivity to components of ensitrelvir or placebo for ensitrelvir
- Known (within 12 months prior to randomization) renal impairment, defined as CrCl &lt; 30 mL/min by Cockcroft-Gault or requiring dialysis
- Known history of cirrhosis or liver decompensation (including ascites, variceal bleeding, or hepatic encephalopathy)

Study Sites: This study was conducted at 193 sites, including 48 in the United States (US), 34 sites in India, 16 sites in Brazil, 15 sites each in Argentina and South Africa, 10 sites each in Pakistan and Philippines, 9 sites in Cambodia, 8 sites in Mexico, 7 sites in Japan, 5 sites in Poland, 4 sites each in Thailand and Uganda, 3 sites each in Ghana and Malawi, and 2 sites in Kenya.

## Randomisation

Participants were randomised 1:1 using permuted block randomisation using an interactive response technology system was used.

When the study was initiated, under protocol version 3.0, randomisation was stratified by geographic region (North America, South America, Europe, Africa, Asia) and risk for severe COVID-19 (high risk, low risk). In protocol version 4.0, the low-risk stratum was changed to 'standard risk.' The definitions of the risk strata can be found in the eligibility criteria.

A third stratification factor was used under protocol versions 4.0 and 5.0: time from symptom onset (≤3 days, and 3 to 5 days, if enrolled under previous versions of the protocol). This stratification factor was deleted in protocol version 6.0 (the final protocol).

## Blinding

Both participants and investigators were blinded to study intervention.

Given a possible decrease in HDL cholesterol with ensitrelvir dosing, HDL and total cholesterol values were not to be reported to the sites or study personnel. Temporary decreases in HDL and total cholesterol did not represent a safety concern. Triglyceride results were reported to sites routinely for all participants as these were considered to have potential safety implications if elevated. In previous studies of ensitrelvir, triglyceride elevations occurred in both treated and placebo treated recipients and were not considered to be unblinding.

The interactive response technology system was programmed with blind-breaking instructions. The date and reason for unblinding were be recorded in the source documentation. The sponsor was to be notified within 24 hours.

Sponsor safety staff could unblind the intervention assignment for any participant with an SAE.

Unblinding was conducted after the database lock for all randomized participants who completed the follow-up at Week 12 to allow analyses relating to the primary, key secondary, and some secondary efficacy and safety objectives to be performed. To ensure that bias was not introduced for objectives evaluated at Week 24, some measures were taken so that investigators and site staff, as well as the sponsor and contract research organization staff directly monitoring sites or handling participant level data, could not access the information on randomized treatment and each participant's individual data.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Eligible adult participants were randomized to receive 1 of the following regimens in the study:

- Ensitrelvir at a dose of 375 mg (3 tablets) for Day 1 and 125 mg (1 tablet) for Days 2 to 5 once daily
- Placebo for ensitrelvir administered once daily for 5 days (Days 1 to 5 [3 matching placebo tablets on Day 1 and 1 matching placebo tablet on Days 2 to 5]).

## Concomitant and rescue therapies

Locally available standard-of-care COVID-19 treatment, including but not limited to mAbs, outpatient IV remdesivir, molnupiravir, convalescent plasma, inhaled budesonide, favipiravir, and fluvoxamine, was permitted after enrolment, as long as not prohibited on the basis of DDI. Any COVID-19 treatment was recorded on a CRF for COVID-19 standard-of-care treatment, including timing of initiation.

## Allowable Symptomatic Relief

The study site did not supply symptomatic relief medication. The use of these symptomatic relief medications for COVID-19 symptom control was allowable at any time during the study. The date and time of administration, as well as the name and dosage regimen, were recorded.

## Prohibited and Permitted Medications

Medications with interactions were not to be restarted until at least the day after completion of study intervention and delayed longer as indicated below and in the drug interaction tool.

Table 52: Restart Timeline for Interacting Drugs

| Interacting drug is a:           | CYP3A substrates   | Sensitive substrate of P-gp or BCRP or OAT3   | Strong or moderate CYP3A inducer; or strong CYP3A inhibitor   |
|----------------------------------|--------------------|-----------------------------------------------|---------------------------------------------------------------|
| When can substrate be restarted? | Study Day 28       | Study Day 6                                   | Study Day 6*                                                  |

BCRP = breast cancer resistance protein; CYP3A = cytochrome P450 3A; DDI = drug-drug interaction; OAT3 = organic anion transporter 3; P-gp = P-glycoprotein

## * Unless inducer or inhibitor is also a CYP3A substrate, then use most conservative

- Day 1 is the day of randomization (ie, first day of ensitrelvir/placebo treatment).
- Due to the long half-life of ensitrelvir and persistent CYP3A inhibition following discontinuation, a slight DDI for CYP3A substrates persists even after Study Day 16.
- Paxlovid (ritonavir-boosted nirmatrelvir), hydroxychloroquine, and ivermectin were prohibited due to DDIs with ensitrelvir.
- Corticosteroids: prednisone and prednisolone were permitted (unless specifically used to treat COVID-19) based on a lack of significant interaction in a clinical DDI study. Furthermore, intranasal or inhaled beclomethasone and fluticasone (Flonase) were allowed.
- Some CYP3A substrates were permitted, and some were prohibited (Note: remdesivir and combined oral contraceptives were permitted).
- Strong CYP3A inducers were prohibited.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

- Organic anion transporter (OAT)-3 substrates with a narrow therapeutic index were prohibited, such as methotrexate.
- P-glycoprotein (P-gp) substrates with a narrow therapeutic index, such as digoxin, and sensitive substrates, such as aliskiren, were prohibited.
- High-dose rosuvastatin (20 to 40 mg) was prohibited, as it is considered a high-dose breast cancer resistance protein (BCRP) substrate. Lower doses were permitted.

## Study assessments

## Efficacy assessment

## Virologic Examination

SARS-CoV-2 infection was defined as laboratory-confirmed active SARS-CoV-2 infection, as determined by a nucleic acid (eg, reverse-transcriptase PCR) or antigen test from any respiratory tract specimen (eg, oropharyngeal, NP or nasal swab, or saliva) collected ≤ 72 hours (3 days) prior to randomization.

## COVID-19 Symptoms

The 15 targeted COVID-19 symptoms were cough, shortness of breath or difficulty breathing, feeling feverish, chills, fatigue, body pain or muscle pain or aches, diarrhea, nausea, vomiting, headache, sore throat, nasal obstruction or congestion, nasal discharge, loss of taste, and loss of smell. Each symptom was scored daily by the participant as absent, mild, moderate, or severe.

Resolution of all targeted symptoms was defined as the first of 2 consecutive days when all targeted symptoms were evaluated as resolved, assessed according to the following rules:

For the pre-existing symptoms that were present prior to COVID-19 onset and considered by the participant to have worsened at baseline (Day 1 diary, completed prior to study intervention initiation), the severity had to improve to be considered resolved.

- Severe at baseline: improved to Moderate, Mild, or Absent postbaseline
- Moderate at baseline: improved to Mild, or Absent postbaseline

(In the event that a participant declared a symptom as Mild at baseline and worsened from prior to COVID-19, the severity had to remain Mild or improved to Absent postbaseline to be considered resolved.)

For the pre-existing symptoms that were present prior to COVID-19 onset and considered by the participant not to have worsened at baseline (preintervention examination), the severity should have remained the same or improved postbaseline to be considered resolved.

- Severe at baseline: Severe, Moderate, Mild, or Absent postbaseline
- Moderate at baseline: Moderate, Mild, or Absent postbaseline
- Mild at baseline: Mild or Absent postbaseline

For symptoms other than the above (symptoms not present prior to COVID-19 onset), the severity should have become or remained Absent.

- Severe or Moderate at baseline: Absent postbaseline
- Mild at baseline: Absent postbaseline

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

- Absent at baseline: Absent postbaseline

## Safety Assessment

Safety was evaluated based on physical examination (including targeted physical examination) findings, vital sign measurements (temperature, pulse, blood pressure, and resting peripheral oxygen saturation), clinical laboratory test results, and AE assessments.

## PK assessment

Plasma samples were collected and used to measure ensitrelvir levels. Pharmacokinetic samples were collected on Day 1 at 60 minutes (± 5 minutes) and 90 minutes (± 5 minutes) postdose, Day 4 (predose and 60 to 90 minutes postdose), and Day 8 (anytime) in a subgroup of 150 evaluable participants.

An additional subgroup of 250 participants were evaluated (to reach a total of 400 for the PK substudy). Pharmacokinetic samples were collected on Day 1 (at 60 minutes [± 5 minutes]) postdose), Day 4 (anytime), and Day 8 (anytime).

## 5.3.2.4.4. Objectives and estimands

## Primary objective

To determine if ensitrelvir reduced the time to sustained symptom resolution through Day 29 among outpatient adults with mild and moderate coronavirus disease 2019 (COVID-19) starting intervention within 3 days of symptom onset.

## Estimand for the primary objective

## Primary endpoint

The comparison between the ensitrelvir treated group versus the placebo group in days from the start of study intervention until sustained resolution of all targeted symptoms (including those occurring prior to COVID-19 infection) and being alive and without hospitalization for any reason by Day 29.

The 15 targeted symptoms were cough, shortness of breath or difficulty breathing, feeling feverish, chills, fatigue, body pain or muscle pain or aches, diarrhea, nausea, vomiting, headache, sore throat, nasal obstruction or congestion, nasal discharge, loss of taste (taste disorder), and loss of smell (smell disorder). Each symptom was scored once daily by the participant as absent, mild, moderate, or severe.

Resolution of all targeted symptoms was defined as the first of 2 consecutive days when all targeted symptoms were evaluated and assessed as resolved.

A treatment policy strategy was used to handle intercurrent events (e.g., irrespective of whether a participant received all doses of S-217622/placebo, mAbs, molnupiravir, outpatient IV remdesivir, favipiravir, fluvoxamine, convalescent plasma, or any other antiviral medications).

A treatment policy strategy was used to handle intercurrent events (e.g., irrespective of whether a participant received all doses of S-217622/placebo, mAbs, molnupiravir, outpatient IV remdesivir, favipiravir, fluvoxamine, convalescent plasma, or any other antiviral medications).

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## Statistical methods for estimation and sensitivity analysis on primary estimands

## Analysis sets

The analysis of the primary endpoint was conducted in the modified Intent-to-Treat (mITT) population, which was initially defined as all randomised participants who took ≥1 dose of S-217622 or placebo. As of protocol version 4.0, patients were also required to have the started the intervention within 3 days of symptom onset.

| Analysis Set                             | Description                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
|------------------------------------------|---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| All Screened Participants (SCR) set      | All participants who provide informed consent for screening for this study.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| All randomized participants (RND) set    | All participants in the SCR set who were randomized to study intervention. For analyses and displays based on the RND set, participants are classified according to randomized intervention.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| Modified intent-to-treat (mITT) set a    | All randomized participants who took ≥ 1 dose of ensitrelvir or placebo and who started intervention within 3 days of symptom onset. For efficacy outcomes, this population was analyzed according to the study intervention the participants were randomized to, regardless of study intervention the participants actually received. This was the primary analysis population.                                                                        |
| Modified intent-to-treat 1 (mITT1) set a | All randomized participants who took ≥ 1 dose of ensitrelvir or placebo. For efficacy outcomes, this population was analyzed according to the study intervention to which the participants were randomized, regardless of the study intervention the participants actually received.                                                                                                                                                                    |
| Viral Culture (VC) set                   | All participants in the mITT population who had documented viral culture at baseline, ie, detectable (> LLoQ) viral culture result at baseline.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| Safety Analysis set (SAF)                | All randomized participants who took ≥ 1 dose of ensitrelvir or the placebo. This population was analyzed according to the study intervention that the participants actually received, rather than the study intervention to which the participants were randomized. If participants received both ensitrelvir and placebo, they were analyzed under the ensitrelvir group, regardless of the number of doses of each study intervention they received. |
| Pharmacokinetic (PK) set                 | All randomized participants who received ≥ 1 dose of ensitrelvir with at least 1 evaluable plasma concentration of ensitrelvir value. This population was used for the drug concentration listing and graphical presentations.                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |

LLoQ = lower limit of quantification

a Study protocol versions 1.0 to 3.0 permitted participants to begin study intervention up to 5 days from symptom onset.

## Statistical analysis of the primary endpoint

Initially, the primary endpoint was to be analysed using Cox regression. In protocol version 4.0, this model was changed to an analysis of the restricted mean symptom duration (with a tau of 27 days) and bias correction of the standard error, along with a 95% confidence interval and a 2-sided p-value.

Follow-up was censored at the appropriate number of days before the day of the last diary record for targeted symptoms if a participant was lost to follow-up or stopped completing their diary. Participants who died or were hospitalized prior to Day 29 remained in the risk-set to Day 28 and were be considered to have not had the symptom resolution event.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## Interim analysis

Three interim analyses of safety data for review by the DSMB were to take place after approximately 25%, 50%, and 75% of the planned enrolment had been completed and followed through to Day 29 (or on a frequency as otherwise recommended by the DSMB).

## Sample size

The target sample size was initially 1490 participants. Allowing for a 5% loss to follow-up, this was expected to provide 1414 participants completing the study. This provided 88.5% power at the 5% significance level to detect a hazard ratio of 0.82 (equivalent to a median time to sustained symptom resolution of 11.5 days) when the placebo median time to sustained resolution is 14 days, assuming exponentially distributed times to symptom resolution.

In protocol version 4.0, the target sample size was increased from 1490 to 2000 participants. The justification was to adjust for a) participants with a symptom duration of 4 or 5 days prior to randomisation enrolled before protocol version 4.0, and b) non-GCP-compliant sites.

In the updated sample size calculation, 1582 participants were expected to provide 90% power at the 5% two-sided significance level to detect a difference in restricted mean symptom duration of 1.5 days in participants who were enrolled within 3 days of symptom onset, based on a restricted mean symptom duration of 13.4 days on placebo, a generalized gamma distribution, and tau=27 (Day 28 is the last point at which sustained symptom resolution can commence and Day 1 is the first day at which symptoms are recorded).

A restricted mean symptom duration of 13.4 days on placebo and a generalized gamma distribution was consistent with data observed in the Phase 3 part of Study 2108T1221 (SCORPIO-SR). Allowing for a 5% loss to follow-up, a total of 1666 evaluable participants enrolled within 3 days of symptom onset was required. Additionally, previous versions of the protocol allowed participants to be enrolled &gt;3 days from symptom onset. To allow for these participants, who would no longer be part of the primary analysis population, a total of approximately 2000 participants were to be enrolled into the trial. This assumed that approximately 334 (17%) of participants will be enrolled &gt;3 days from symptom onset. Participants who were not evaluable as part of the primary analysis population due to Good Clinical Practice violations could be replaced.

## Secondary objectives

## First key secondary objective

- To determine the effect of ensitrelvir compared with placebo on the change from baseline in quantitative log10 severe acute respiratory syndrome coronavirus 2 (SARS-CoV-2) ribonucleic acid (RNA) levels by polymerase chain reaction (PCR) on nasopharyngeal (NP) swab at Day 4 among outpatient adults with SARS-CoV-2 starting intervention within 3 days of symptom onset.

## Estimands for the secondary objectives

## First key secondary endpoint

Change from baseline in quantitative log10 SARS-CoV-2 RNA levels by PCR in nasopharyngeal swab at Day 4 (mITT set).

The estimand framework was not used.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## Statistical methods for estimation and sensitivity analysis

## Statistical analysis of the first key secondary endpoint

Analysis of covariance (ANCOVA) was used to analyse the first key secondary endpoint. The covariates were baseline log10 SARS-CoV-2 RNA and treatment group. Participants with missing data were excluded from the analysis (the missingness was assumed to be missingness completely at random).

## Multiplicity/type-1-error control

The first key secondary endpoint was tested only if the results for the primary endpoint were statistically significant.

## Protocol Violations/Deviations

A total of 1369 participants in mITT set had critical/major protocol deviations from Day 1 through the entire study (final analysis): 682 in the ensitrelvir group and 687 in the placebo group.

The common critical/major protocol deviations were (ensitrelvir vs. placebo):

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

- laboratory assessments (538 vs. 547)
- patient reported outcomes (293 vs. 279)
- study procedures (34 vs. 36)
- visit schedule (31 vs. 28)
- informed consent and process (31 vs. 24)
- administration of prohibited concomitant medications (21 vs. 18)
- deviation of the inclusion criteria (11 vs. 11)
- deviation of the exclusion criteria (9 vs. 8)
- IP administration (9 vs. 8).

No notable differences were found in the number of participants with critical/major protocol deviations across the study intervention groups.

## Deviations from study plan

Participant recruitment started under protocol version 3 (dated 23 June 2022). The protocol was subsequently amended 3 times. These most important changes are summarised below.

## Protocol Version 4.0, Dated 17 Feb 2023

- Revised description of 'low-risk' participants to 'standard-risk' to reflect the current epidemiology.
- As requested by FDA, only standard-risk participants will be enrolled in the US.
- Reduced the enrolment symptom window from 5 days to within 3 days of symptom onset.
- Revised the definition of sustained symptom resolution (primary outcome measure) as the first of 2 consecutive days (instead of 4 days) without symptoms.
- The primary efficacy analysis was changed from Cox regression to restricted mean symptom duration up to Day 28.
- Adjusted the primary analysis population to only include those participants enrolled within 3 days of symptom onset (mITT population), and revised primary, secondary, and exploratory objectives/outcomes/estimands accordingly.
- The order in which secondary endpoints should be tested was deleted from the protocol (the order was deferred to the statistical analysis plan).
- Eliminated the 50% enrolment caps for standard-risk and high-risk.
- Changed the primary endpoint to include 15 instead of 13 COVID-19 symptoms. The 2 new symptoms were loss of smell and loss of taste.
- Clarified that hypertension, cardiovascular disease, and chronic lung disease requiring daily prescribed therapy are considered for the high-risk definition.
- Clarified in the Inclusion Criteria for high-risk participants that known chronic kidney disease is defined as a known CrCl or eGFR &lt; 60 mL/min/1.73 m2 within the past 12 months (which is allowed, as long as the participant does not have known CrCl &lt; 30 mL/min)

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

- Changed the sample size to target 90% statistical power and adjust for a) participants enrolled before the protocol amendment with a symptom duration of 4 or 5 days prior to randomization, and b) non GCP-compliant sites.

## Protocol Version 5.0, Dated 24 Feb 2023

No important changes.

## Protocol Version 6.0, Dated 25 Oct 2023

- Randomisation will no longer be stratified by time from symptom onset, and the efficacy analyses will no longer be adjusted for this.
- Added two new key secondary objectives: 'To determine the effect of S-217622 compared with placebo on the occurrence of persistent and late-onset symptoms of COVID-19 at Week 12' among outpatient adults with SARS-CoV-2 starting intervention within 3 days of symptom onset and among all outpatient adults with SARS-CoV-2, respectively.
- Added 'including persistent and/or late-onset symptoms' to the list of secondary treatment efficacy measures and added 'persistent and late-onset symptoms' as examples of long-term sequelae.

Database lock: 15 March 2024.

Unblinding: 18 March 2024.

## Outcomes and estimation

## Primary efficacy endpoint

For the primary analysis, the time to sustained resolution of all 15 targeted symptoms, being alive and without hospitalisation by Day 29 in the mITT set in the ensitrelvir group was compared with the placebo group.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

CI = confidence interval; N = number of participants in analysis set; n = number of participants; % = based on N

The time to sustained symptom resolution is defined as the first day of 2 consecutive days with complete resolution of COVID-19 symptoms on participant self-assessment and being alive and without hospitalization for any reason by Day 29.

Participants who died or were hospitalized from any cause prior to Day 29 remained in the risk-set to Day 28 and were considered to have not had sustained symptom resolution with time to event censored at Day 28. For other participants without sustained symptom resolution, censoring of follow-up occurred on

the last day that the outcome could have been achieved. Specifically, as 2 consecutive days of meeting the endpoint criteria was required, censoring occurred 1 day before the last day of completion of the diary card. If a participant had none of the targeted symptoms evaluated at any time during follow-up and was not known to have died or been hospitalized, the participant was excluded from the analysis.

Restricted mean symptom duration compares the time(days) from the start of intervention to sustained resolution. A tau of 27 was used in the analysis.

All the targeted COVID-19 symptom (cough, shortness of breath or difficulty breathing, feeling feverish, chills, fatigue, body pain or muscle pain or aches, diarrhea, nausea, vomiting, headache, sore throat, nasal obstruction or congestion, nasal discharge, loss of taste, loss of smell).

## Ancillary analyses

## Hospitalisation and death

The number of participants with hospitalisation due to COVID-19 or death due to any cause through Day 29 in the mITT set was 3 (0.3 %) in the ensitrelvir group and 1 (0.1%) in the placebo group. The risk ratio estimate comparing the probability of hospitalisation due to COVID-19 or death due to any cause between study intervention to placebo for the ensitrelvir vs placebo groups was 2.9937 (95% CI: 0.3120, 28.7278) with 2-sided nominal p-value of 0.6246.

## Analysis of time to resolution of 6 COVID-19 symptoms

The number of alive participants with sustained resolution of 6 targeted symptoms (nasal obstruction or congestion, nasal discharge, sore throat, cough, feeling feverish, and fatigue) without hospitalisation for any reason through Day 29 in the mITT set was 807 (85.4%) in the ensitrelvir group and 784 (83.1%) in the placebo group.

The estimate of the difference in restricted mean symptom durations (95% CI) for the ensitrelvir vs placebo groups in the mITT set was -0.7 days (-1.49, 0.05) with nominal p-value of 0.0671. In a supplementary analysis, a Peto-Prentice's stratified generalized Wilcoxon test gave a 2-sided nominal p-value of 0.0561.

## Viral and symptomatic rebound

In the mITT set, no participant in either treatment group was reported to have symptomatic viral rebound from Day 6 up to Day 29. Symptomatic viral rebound was defined as either an increase in quantitative NP SARS-CoV-2 viral culture or NP SARS-CoV-2 RNA levels by quantitative PCR in the setting of new or worsening clinical symptoms.

Viral RNA rebound rates were calculated based on the ≥ 0.5 log10 RNA copies/mL and the ≥ 1.0 log10 RNA copies/mL criteria following the peak viral load.

Based on the ≥ 0.5 log10 RNA copies/mL criterion, the proportion of participants with viral RNA rebound from Day 4 or Days 8 to 16 in mITT population was numerically higher in the ensitrelvir group (9.3%; 86/929) than in the placebo group (6.4%; 59/929).

Of those participants with viral RNA rebound, 3/76 participants in the ensitrelvir and 4/55 participants in the placebo group had infectious virus above the limit of detection.

The number of participants with persistent and/or late-onset symptoms of COVID-19 (Week 12) in the mITT set was 227 (24.0%) in the ensitrelvir group and 221 (23.4%) in the placebo group. The estimated risk ratio (95% CI) for the ensitrelvir vs placebo groups in the mITT set was 1.02 (0.872, 1.205) with a nominal p-value of 0.7869.

These analyses were conducted in the modified intention-to-treat population with less than 72 hours from symptom onset to randomisation. For each symptom, the participants who had the symptom at baseline (excluding preexisting symptoms before COVID-19 that did not change at baseline) were included in the analysis. The median time to resolution was estimated using the Kaplan-Meier method. P-values were calculated using the stratified PetoPrentice's generalized Wilcoxon test.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## 5.3.2.6. SCORPIO-PEP (2206T1331)

## 5.3.2.6.1. Study title

A Phase 3 randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled study to evaluate the efficacy and safety of S217622 in the prevention of symptomatic SARS-CoV-2 infection in household contacts of an individual with symptomatic COVID-19 (SCORPIO-PEP study)

## 5.3.2.6.2. Study design

This study was a Phase 3 randomized, double-blind, multicentre, parallel-group, placebo-controlled comparative study in participants who were household members of symptomatic severe acute respiratory syndrome coronavirus (SARS-CoV-2)-infected index patients. Participants were randomly assigned in a 1:1 ratio to receive study intervention (ensitrelvir or placebo).

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Figure 13: Study schema

<!-- image -->

Event-driven Visit: Days 1 to 28 c

COVID-19 = coronavirus disease 2019; D = Day; V = Visit

- a Visit 1, Day 1 for the index patient will not necessarily be the same day as Visit 1, Day 1 for the participant. However, the participant must provide informed consent ≤ 72 hours from onset of COVID-19 symptoms (see Appendix 6 [Section 10.6]) in the index patient and ≤ 1 calendar day from the time that the index patient provides consent.
- b If a participant qualifies for the study, study intervention will be administered on site at the end of the Screening Visit immediately after randomization (if home-visit medical care is deployed, the first dose should be administered immediately after randomization in the home).
- c After participants are enrolled, the investigator will initiate an Event-driven Visit if clinically indicated on Days 1 through 28 or in the event of COVID-19 symptoms onset as the occurrence (or worsening in the case of preexisting COVID-19-like symptoms) of ≥ 1 of the 14 specified COVID-19 symptoms for at least 48 hours. The 14 specified COVID-19 symptoms are (1) fever (defined as body temperature ≥ 38.0°C per tympanic or rectal thermometer or ≥ 37.5°C per axillary, oral, or forehead/temporal thermometer; see Section 8.2.3) (2) shortness of breath or difficulty breathing, (3) cough, (4) sore throat, (5) nasal congestion or runny nose, (6) chills, (7) fatigue, (8) body or muscle pain or aches, (9) headache, (10) nausea, (11) vomiting, (12) diarrhea, (13) change in sense of taste, and (14) change in sense of smell.

If an Event-driven Visit occurs within the allowable timeframe for Days 3, 6, 10, 15, 21, or 28, the specified assessments on Days 3, 6, 10, 15, 21, or 28 can be performed at the same visit. A study participant can have multiple Event-driven Visits if COVID-19 symptom onset definition is met multiple times.

After the initial Event-driven Visit, additional Event-driven Visits will be triggered if new symptom(s) meet the criteria of symptom onset definition after all other COVID-19 symptoms resolved (based on the symptoms resolution definition in the study protocol).

## Study population

Key inclusion criteria

Index patient (patient with SARS-CoV-2 Infection, 1 per household)

- o Paediatric patients or adult patients (of any age)
- o Must have had at least 1 COVID-19 symptom as listed below within 24 hours before the index patient provided informed consent. COVID-19 symptom(s) must have been deemed by the

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

investigator as related to the current SARS-CoV-2 infection (not related to pre-existing comorbidities) or deemed as pre-existing and worsened due to SARS-CoV-2 infection.

- Fever (defined as body temperature ≥ 38.0°C per tympanic or rectal thermometer or ≥ 37.5°C per axillary, oral, or forehead/temporal thermometer)
- Shortness of breath or difficulty breathing
- Cough
- Sore throat
- Nasal congestion or runny nose
- Chills
- Fatigue
- Body or muscle pain or aches
- Headache
- Nausea
- Vomiting
- Diarrhoea
- Change in sense of taste
- Change in sense of smell
- o Must have had a positive SARS-CoV-2 test (a nucleic acid amplification test or antigen test) from any respiratory tract specimen (eg, oropharyngeal, NP or nasal swab, or saliva) from a sample collected ≤ 72 hours prior to randomization of the participant
- o Must have a potential study participant who can participate in the study within 72 hours after onset of COVID-19 symptoms in an index patient

Study participants (multiple study participants from the same household were allowed to be enrolled):

- o Must have been ≥ 12 years of age
- o Must have had a negative screening for SARS-CoV-2 infection as determined by SARS-CoV-2 test, a nucleic acid amplification test or antigen test from any respiratory tract specimen (eg, oropharyngeal, NP or nasal swab, or saliva) at the local laboratory.
- o Must have lived in a household with the index patient and continued to live in same household and shared common areas such as dining rooms and bathrooms from within 72 hours after onset of COVID-19 symptoms in the index patient through the end of the study
- o Must not have been considered by the investigator to have had SARS-CoV-2 infection and:
- had no measured fever at Screening
- had no COVID-19 symptoms at Screening
- o Were capable and willing to complete a participant diary

## Key exclusion criteria

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Index patients were excluded if the following applied:

Had documented respiratory infection other than COVID-19 within 14 days prior to the Screening visit

Study participants were excluded if the following applied:

- o Had documented respiratory infection other than COVID-19 within 14 days prior to the Screening visit
- o Tested positive for SARS-CoV-2 in the last 6 months
- o Had an underlying disease requiring systemic corticosteroids (excluding topical) or antipyretics/analgesics
- o Known renal impairment
- o Had severe liver dysfunction, such as known history of cirrhosis or liver decompensation

## Study Sites

This was a multicentre study conducted at 178 contracted sites, including 95 sites in the US, 67 sites in Japan, 2 sites in Vietnam, 11 Sites in Argentina, and 3 sites in South Africa.

## Randomisation

Participants were randomly assigned in a 1:1 ratio, stratified by time from symptom onset in the index patient to enrollment of the study participant (&lt;48hours, ≥48 hours) and geographic region (North America, South America, Europe, Africa, Japan, Asia excluding Japan). An interactive web response system (IWRS) was used.

## Blinding

This was a double-blind study in which participants and investigators were blinded to study intervention.

The IWRS was programmed with blind-breaking instructions. In case of an emergency, the investigator had the sole responsibility for determining whether a participant should be unblinded. The date and reason for unblinding were recorded.

Sponsor safety staff may have unblinded the intervention assignment for any participant with an SAE. If the SAE required that an expedited regulatory report be sent to 1 or more regulatory agencies, a copy of the report, identifying the participant's intervention assignment, was sent to investigators in accordance with local regulations and/or sponsor policy.

Laboratory/analyte results that could unblind the study were not reported to study sites or other blinded personnel until the study had been unblinded. Given possible decreases in high-density lipoprotein cholesterol (HDL-C) with ensitrelvir dosing, HDL-C was not reported to the sites. These observed laboratory changes were temporary and transient and did not represent a safety concern.

## Description of trial intervention

Eligible participants were randomised to receive 1 of the following regimens in the study:

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

- Ensitrelvir at a dose of 375 mg (3 tablets) on Day 1 and 125 mg (1 tablet) for Days 2 to 5 once daily
- Placebo for ensitrelvir administered once daily for 5 days (Days 1 to 5 [3 matching placebo tablets on Day 1 and 1 matching placebo tablet on Days 2 to 5]).

The first dose of study intervention was taken at the study site on Day 1 (if home-visit medical care was deployed, the first dose was administered immediately after randomisation in the home). All subsequent doses (ie, Days 2 to 5) were self-administered outside the study site (e.g. at home). The second dose was taken 24 ± 8 hours after the first dose, allowing the participant to select a convenient 24-hour dosing schedule thereafter to complete a total of 5 doses.

## Concomitant and rescue therapies

## Prohibited and Precautionary Medications

Use of the following therapies was prohibited from the time of informed consent through 28 days after the first dose of study intervention:

- Sensitive CYP3A substrates with narrow therapeutic index. (Note: remdesivir and combined oral contraceptives are permitted).

Use of the following therapies was prohibited from the time of informed consent through 10 days after the last dose of study intervention. However, even if a prohibited concomitant therapy was used, the participant should have continued the study procedures.

- Hydroxychloroquine, and ivermectin were prohibited due to DDIs
- Paxlovid (nirmatrelvir/ritonavir): Given the limited safety information of coadministration of ensitrelvir and Paxlovid this was not allowed. If Paxlovid was administered after the treatment period of ensitrelvir, which was only anticipated in the case the study participant tested positive for SARS-CoV-2 and met the study definition for COVID-19 symptoms onset, careful monitoring with caution of therapeutic and adverse effects were highly recommended due to the aforementioned potential increase of nirmatrelvir AUC and Cmax.
- Corticosteroids administered via any route (including intranasal, inhaled, oral, intra-articular but excluding topical).
- o However, the following were permitted: prednisolone was permitted (unless specifically used to treat COVID-19); intranasal or inhaled fluticasone was permitted (unless specifically used to treat COVID-19); intranasal or inhaled beclomethasone; dexamethasone could be administered the day after the last dose of study intervention.
- CYP3A substrates except for sensitive CYP3A substrates with narrow therapeutic index.

Use of the following therapies was prohibited from the time of informed consent through the day after the last study intervention.

- Strong CYP3A inducers.
- Methotrexate as organic anion transporter 3 substrate with a narrow therapeutic index.
- Digoxin and dabigatran as P-glycoprotein substrates with a narrow therapeutic index.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

- High dose of rosuvastatin (20 to 40 mg, as it is considered breast cancer resistance protein substrate and ensitrelvir could have influenced the treatment by rosuvastatin when high dose of rosuvastatin is administered).

## Rescue Medications

The use of symptomatic-relief medications for COVID-19 symptom control (such as aspirin, ibuprofen, acetaminophen, other nonsteroidal inflammatory drugs, zinc, dietary supplements, herbal remedies, decongestants, cough suppressants, and antihistamines) were allowable at any time after symptoms develop.

The participant was encouraged to start symptomatic-relief medications only after SARS-CoV-2 positive infection confirmation. Symptomatic-relief medication for prophylaxis use was prohibited.

Index patients could have received any locally available standard-of-care COVID-19 therapies as indicated in their regions.

Participants who tested positive for SARS-CoV-2 infection during the study and met the study definition for COVID-19 symptoms onset could have received any locally standard-of-care COVID-19 therapies available and indicated in their regions, as long as compatible with study intervention.

## Study assessments

## Efficacy assessments

## Virology Test

The investigator or designee collected a nasopharyngeal (NP) swab from index patients and participants.

The amount of viral RNA was measured by RT-PCR (central laboratory confirmed) and positive or negative results were determined. Spike gene, nonstructural protein 5 (NSP5) and its cleavage site sequence analyses and phenotypic assay were conducted using the NP swab collected.

In NP samples with positive SARS-CoV-2 RNA, influenza RNA was measured to investigate coinfections.

## COVID-19 Symptoms

Participants were asked to keep a log of COVID-19 symptoms in a study diary. On Day 1, participants completed the study diary prior to initiating ensitrelvir or placebo and during the evening. Participants were asked to complete entries twice daily on their own from Days 1 to 28. The diary was to be completed at approximately the same time every day.

Participants were asked to measure their body temperature twice daily from Days 1 to 28 and enter the temperature in the diary. When acetaminophen was administered, it was recommended that the participant waited 4 hours prior to COVID-19 symptoms diary recording or body temperature check.

Fever was defined as:

- ≥ 38.0°C per tympanic or rectal thermometer

- ≥ 37.5°C per axillary, oral, or forehead/temporal thermometer

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

COVID-19 symptoms were scored on a 4-point scale (none, mild, moderate, or severe) except for change in the sense of taste and smell which was scored on a 3-point scale (same as usual, less than usual, no sense).

## Staff Review of COVID-19 Symptoms Diary

The study diary was reviewed by study staff in person or remotely with each participant. Participants who reported worsening symptoms from any cause during the trial were referred to their healthcare provider or closest emergency room, as medically indicated.

## Viral RNA/Symptom Rebound

Using viral RNA from NP swabs and symptoms from participants symptom diary, the proportion of participants who had viral RNA and/or COVID-19 symptom rebound was calculated. The viral RNA and/or COVID-19 symptom rebound was defined in the statistical analysis plan (SAP).

## Safety assessment

Any test results which were considered to be clinically significant by the investigator were to be recorded as AEs. If an abnormal laboratory finding was associated with disease or organ toxicity, the investigator reported only the disease or organ toxicity as an AE.

For any abnormal laboratory test results (haematology, blood chemistry) or other safety assessments (e.g., physical examination) that worsen following exposure to the study intervention from baseline, the investigator considered whether those results were clinically significant. Abnormal laboratory test results were defined as values outside the reference range. For test results which were abnormal at baseline and significantly worsen following the initiation of the study, the investigator also considered whether those results were clinically significant.

In addition, when any test results met the management and stopping criteria for liver function abnormalities, the results of further assessments and required follow-up were recorded in the Liver Event Form.

## Pharmacokinetic Assessment

Pharmacokinetic samples were collected at Visits 2 (anytime) and 3 (anytime), and the Event-driven Visit. For the PK analysis set, plasma concentrations of ensitrelvir were documented.

## 5.3.2.6.3. Objectives and estimands

## Primary objective

To compare the ensitrelvir with placebo in the prevention of symptomatic SARS-CoV-2 infection in participants with a negative screening SARS-CoV-2 infection who are household members (hereinafter referred to as 'participants') of SARS-CoV-2-infected patients (hereinafter referred to as 'index patients') through Day 10 (using the mITT analysis set).

The superiority of ensitrelvir versus placebo was evaluated.

## Estimand for the primary objective

Primary endpoint Proportion of participants in the mITT analysis set with a negative screening SARS-CoV-2 infection (central laboratory-confirmed [CLC] negative RT-PCR test) who are infected with SARS-CoV-2 (CLCpositive RT-PCR test) and have COVID-19 symptoms onset through Day 10.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Intercurrent events:

- Withdrawal of study intervention due to use of rescue or prohibited medication (treatment policy strategy)
- Death or hospitalisation from any cause (composite strategy: assumed to have been infected with SARS-CoV-2 with symptoms)

## Statistical methods for estimation and sensitivity analysis on primary estimand

## Analysis sets

The efficacy analyses were conducted in the modified intention-to-treat (mITT) set, which included all randomised participants who:

1. had a negative screening SARS-CoV-2 infection, confirmed by RT-PCR result (central laboratory) collected from a sample prior to the first dose of randomized treatment (Visit 1),
2. were associated with index patients who did not have an exclusionary protocol deviation, AND
3. had at least 1 dose of either ensitrelvir or placebo.

Criterion 2 was added in protocol version 5.0.

Criterion 3 was not specified in the protocol but in the statistical analysis plan (as of version 2.2, dated 24-July-2024).

| Participant Analysis Set   | Description                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
|----------------------------|--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| ITT analysis set           | All participants randomly assigned to study intervention.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| mITT analysis set          | All participants randomly assigned to study intervention who: 1. also had a negative screening SARS-CoV-2 infection, confirmed by RT-PCR result (central laboratory) collected from a sample prior to the first dose of randomized treatment (Visit 1); and, 2. were associated with index patients who did not have an exclusionary protocol deviation. 3. Had at least1 dose of either ensitrelvir or placebo. Participants were analyzed according to the assigned (randomized) study intervention. |
| Safety analysis set        | All participants randomly assigned to study intervention who received at least 1 dose of study intervention. Participants were analyzed with the ensitrelvir arm if they had received any dose of the active treatment, while participants in the placebo group had received placebo for all doses.                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| PK analysis set            | All participants who receive at least 1 dose of ensitrelvir and have at least 1 evaluable concentration of ensitrelvir in plasma; this analysis set was used for the concentration listing and graphical presentations.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## Statistical analysis of the primary endpoint

The primary analysis was a generalised estimating equations approach applied via a Poisson regression model. The model contained terms for the study intervention (ensitrelvir or placebo) and randomisation stratification factors as independent variables. An exchangeable structure was assumed for the covariance matrix to account for correlation between participants from the same household. A sandwich estimator was used for robust inference. Participant, study intervention, time from index participant symptoms, and geographic region were treated as class variables.

Participants who withdrew from the study before occurrence of the event for the primary endpoint were assumed to have been infected with SARS-CoV-2 with symptoms.

## Multiplicity/type-1-error control

The analysis of the primary endpoint in the ITT population was performed only if the analysis in the mITT population was statistically significant at the 2-sided significance level of 0.05.

The secondary endpoints were not multiplicity controlled.

## Interim analyses

No formal interim analysis was conducted in this study; however, regular safety reviews were undertaken at preplanned enrollment milestones by an independent data safety monitoring board (DSMB).

## Sample size

To ensure 92 events (i.e. the number of SARS-CoV-2 positive participants who develop COVID-19 symptoms), approximately 2200 participants were expected to be randomised to study intervention in 1:1 ratio. However, protocol version 5.0 (the final version) stated that the total number of participants could be adjusted to achieve 94 events.

For the analysis of the primary endpoint, a total of 92 participants who experience an event (event defined as meeting definition of the primary endpoint) within the primary analysis population was required to detect the difference between S-217622 and placebo in the proportion of participants who experience the event using an exact conditional test with at least 90% power at a 2-sided significance level of 0.05. This is assuming that the proportion in the placebo group is 7.0%, the risk ratio (vs. placebo) is 0.5, and the number of event occurrences follows the Poisson distribution.

## Secondary objectives

## Key secondary

- Same as primary objective but analysed in the ITT analysis set (instead of in the mITT set)

## Secondary

- To compare ensitrelvir with placebo in the prevention of SARS-CoV-2 infection in participants (defined as CLC-positive RT-PCR test)

The superiority of ensitrelvir versus placebo was evaluated.

## Estimand for the secondary objective

Key secondary endpoint Same as the primary endpoint but analysed in the ITT set instead of the mITT set.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Other secondary endpoints

Proportion of participants who are infected with SARS-CoV-2 (CLC-positive RT-PCR test) through Days 10, 15, 21, or 28, regardless of occurrence of COVID-19 symptoms

## Statistical methods for estimation and sensitivity analysis on the secondary estimands

Statistical analysis of the secondary endpoints

The proportion of participants infected with SARS-CoV-2 (CLC-positive RT-PCR test) at days 10, 15, 21, and 28, regardless of occurrence of COVID-19 symptoms, was analysed the same way as the primary endpoint.

- c. mITT population: Participants randomly assigned to study intervention and 1) had a confirmed negative RT-PCR test prior to first dose of randomized treatment, 2) were associated with index patients with no exclusionary protocol deviations and 3) had at least1 dose of either ensitrelvir or placebo.
- d. The ITTBP population was defined as the subset of ITT population participants with a positive screening result for SARS-CoV-2, as assessed by RT-PCR at the central lab.
- e. Per protocol population: Participants in the mITT population without any exclusionary major protocol deviations.
- f. Pharmacokinetic population: Participants who received at least 1 dose of ensitrelvir and had at least 1 evaluable concentration of ensitrelvir in plasma.
- g. This category consisted of treated participants who reported taking fewer doses of ensitrelvir or placebo than have been provided in the assigned kit but did not have a completed Discontinuation of Study Intervention eCRF.

## Deviations from study plan

Participant enrolment started under protocol version 3.0 (dated 19 April 2023). The protocol was subsequently amended 2 times. The most important changes are summarised below.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## Protocol Version 4.0, Dated 03 Nov 2023

- A DSMB was added to review ongoing safety data.

## Protocol Version 5.0, Dated 08 Jul 2024

- Clarified that the sample size may vary from the original estimate to achieve the targeted number of events.
- Added exclusion criterion that participants cannot live in a household with ≥10 people.
- Clarified that mITT analysis set definition includes participants who are associated with index patients who meet the inclusion criteria.

Database lock: 21 October 2024

Unblinding: 21 October 2024

## Outcomes and estimation

## Primary efficacy analysis

For the primary analysis, the proportion of participants (95% CI) with symptomatic COVID-19 through Day 10 in the mITT analysis set (i.e., confirmed to be SARS-CoV-2 negative at screening) in the ensitrelvir group was compared with the placebo group (see table below).

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Table 67: Risk for Symptomatic COVID-19 Through Day 10 (Primary Analysis, mITT Analysis Set)

|                         | Ensitrelvir Group N = 1030 n (%)   | Placebo Group N = 1011 n (%)   | Total N = 2041 n (%)   |
|-------------------------|------------------------------------|--------------------------------|------------------------|
| Endpoint Status         |                                    |                                |                        |
| No symptomatic COVID-19 | 1000 (97.1)                        | 920 (91.0)                     | 1920 (94.1)            |
| Symptomatic COVID-19    | 30 (2.9)                           | 91 (9.0)                       | 121 (5.9)              |
| [95% CI] a              | [1.97, 4.13]                       | [7.31, 10.94]                  | [4.94, 7.04]           |
| Risk Ratio b            | 0.33                               |                                |                        |
| [95% CI] c              | [0.22, 0.49]                       |                                |                        |
| p-value d               | < 0.0001                           |                                |                        |

CI = confidence interval; CLC = central laboratory-confirmed; COVID-19 = coronavirus disease 2019; GEE = generalized estimating equations; mITT = modified intention-to-treat; RT-PCR = reverse transcription polymerase chain reaction

Symptomatic COVID-19 was defined as a CLC-positive RT-PCR test and the occurrence (or worsening [increasing a symptom score from baseline] in the case of pre-existing COVID-19-like symptoms) of ≥ 1 of the 14 specified COVID-19 symptoms for at least 48 hours.

- a. Confidence intervals for proportion of participants with symptomatic COVID-19 use the ClopperPearson method.
- b. Risk ratio (relative risk) of symptomatic COVID-19 in participants randomized to ensitrelvir vs participants randomized to placebo from GEE Poisson regression model.
- c. Confidence interval calculated from GEE Poisson regression model.
- d. p-value for the log coefficient of treatment effect equal to zero in GEE Poisson regression model.

## Ancillary analyses

## Hospitalisation and death

The proportion of participants with hospitalisation, defined as ≥ 24 hours of acute care for any reason through Day 28 in the mITT analysis set was 2/1030 (0.2%) in the ensitrelvir group and 0% in the placebo group. There were no deaths for any reason through Day 28.

## 5.3.3. Treatment-emergent amino acid substitutions in the Phase 3 trials

Ensitrelvir treatment-emergent amino acid substitutions (ENS TEAAS) in M pro were defined in studies SCORPIO-SR (2108T1221), SCORPIO-HR (ACTIV-2d/A5407) and SCORPIO-PEP (2206T1331) as the detection of an amino acid substitution in M pro with a mutation frequency of ≥ 15% in ≥ 3 participants receiving ensitrelvir and at a proportion ≥ 2.5-fold higher than that seen in participants receiving placebo.

The populations referred to below are defined as follows:

- mITT (2206T1331): All participants randomly assigned to study intervention who:
- o also had a negative screening SARS-CoV-2 infection test, confirmed by RT-PCR result (central laboratory) collected from a sample prior to the first dose of randomized treatment (Visit 1); and,
- o were associated with index patients who did not have an exclusionary protocol deviation.
- o Had at least one dose of either ensitrelvir or placebo.
- ITTBP (2206T1331): The subset of ITT analysis set participants with a positive baseline screening result for SARS-CoV-2, with the test sample collected on or before the date of randomization, assessed by RT-PCR at the central laboratory.
- mITT2 (ACTIV-2d/A5407): All randomized participants who took ≥ 1 dose of ensitrelvir or placebo and who started intervention within 3 days of symptom onset with a positive baseline PCR result.

In 2108T1221 (Phase 3 part), a total of 207 participants had the paired sequence data available at pre- and post-dose out of 345 participants randomised and treated with ensitrelvir 375/125 mg in the ITT population with less than 72 hours from COVID-19 onset to randomization. Out of the 207 participants, treatment-emergent amino acid substitutions (TEAASs) in nsp5 were detected in 20 participants. As TEAASs confirmed reduced susceptibility to ensitrelvir in in vitro analysis, M49L and S144A were detected in 12 and 2 participants, respectively. In addition, M49I was also detected in 3 participants (including 1 participant with M49L, M49I and T25A).

In the SCORPIO-HR study (ACTIV-2d/A5407), a total of 273 participants had paired nsp5 sequence data available at pre- and post-dose out of 767 participants randomized and treated with ensitrelvir in the mITT2 set, which was defined as all randomized participants who received at least one dose of ensitrelvir or placebo within 3 days of symptom onset with a confirmed positive baseline PCR result (by central laboratory). Out of the 273 participants, TEAASs in nsp5 were detected in 23 participants.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

TEAAS of M49L was detected in 13 participants, M49I was also detected in 3 participants (including 2 participants with M49L and M49I).

In the SCORPIO-PEP study (2206T1331), a total of 51 participants had nsp5 sequence data available at post-dose out of 243 participants with a positive PCR post-dose (central laboratory) in the ensitrelvir group of the mITT population, 1030 participants. Out of 51 participants, the ensitrelvir TEAASs in the nsp5 were detected in 19 participants: M49L in 10 participants, T25A in 4 participants, M49I in 3 participants and P52S in 3 participants (including 1 participant with M49L and T25A).

Viruses with the amino acid substitutions T25A and M49L were associated with 14.4- and 41.0-fold reductions in ensitrelvir susceptibility compared with wild type. Other amino acid substitutions had the following fold changes compared to wild type T25I (2.9), T25N (4.84), M49I (5.25), P52S (5.5), and S144A (11) (see Primary Pharmacodynamics Section).

None of the ENS TEAAS that conferred reduced ensitrelvir susceptibility caused any significant change in either remdesivir or nirmatrelvir susceptibility. Similarly, the substitutions T21I + E166V and L50F + E166V, which confer a 36.0- to 43.4-fold reduction in nirmatrelvir susceptibility, did not significantly alter ensitrelvir susceptibility (see Primary Pharmacodynamic Section).
