## 5.3.5. Analysis performed across trials (pooled analyses and metaanalysis)

Due to increasing vaccination rates, natural immunity and potentially less pathogenic variants, the risk of hospitalization among patients with COVID-19 was considerably lower during the conduct of the ensitrelvir studies (Studies 2108T1221 and ACTIV 2d/A5407) than in the earlier EPIC-SR and EPIC-HR studies that evaluated nirmatrelvir plus ritonavir. As such, demonstrating robust randomized clinical trial data for ensitrelvir on the impact of reducing hospitalization was not possible.

There have been a number of published studies that have aimed to correlate viral load reduction and clearance with clinical outcomes (Elias et al, 2024; Parienti and de Grooth, 2022; Singh et al, 2024).

In the study by Elias and colleagues, the authors used a meta-analytical approach to integrate data from a systematic search of the literature and identified a correlation between the virological effect of the different COVID-19 treatments following treatment and the corresponding clinical efficacy of these treatments on hospitalization.

Although it is an indirect comparison, and differences exist between the studies included in the analysis by Elias and colleagues (namely the immune background of patients and the circulating variants), an analysis of ensitrelvir viral reduction data was conducted post-publication using the meta-regression model.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Ensitrelvir viral load reduction data were evaluated in Study 2108T1221 (Phase 2a, Phase 2b, Phase 2b/3, and Phase 3 parts) and plotted on the correlation curve in comparison to those of other COVID 19 small molecule antivirals, convalescent plasma, and mAbs (Figure 14). Viral load reductions for ensitrelvir were greater than, or similar to, reductions observed with nirmatrelvir plus ritonavir, but greater than those observed with molnupiravir.

Using the meta-regression model from the analysis by Elias and colleagues (Elias et al, 2024), the estimated clinical efficacy of ensitrelvir for risk reduction in hospitalization/death ranged from 80% (95% CI: 61%, 92%) to 96% (95% CI: 84%, 99%) on Day 3 and from 67% (95% CI: 45%, 83%) to 90% (95% CI: 73%, 97%) on Day 5. In comparison, the reported clinical efficacy was 89% (95% CI, 72%, 96%) (Hammond et al, 2022) for nirmatrelvir plus ritonavir and 30% (95% CI, 1%, 51%) (Jayk Bernal et al, 2022) and 42% (95% CI, -2%, 83%) (Caraco et al, 2022) for molnupiravir.

Figure 14: Estimated Correlation Curve Between Clinical Efficacy for Risk Reduction in Hospitalization or Death and Viral RNA Reduction: Plotted Results of Ensitrelvir Studies

<!-- image -->

CI = confidence interval; RNA = ribonucleic acid

The X axis shows the virological effect defined by the difference (treatment minus placebo) in change from baseline, and the Y axis shows clinical efficacy defined by the relative risk of hospitalization or death.

For the ensitrelvir studies, the 95% CIs (error bars) on the X axis were calculated for the virological effect based on data from ensitrelvir studies, and the 95% CIs on the Y axis were extrapolated based on the upper/lower limit of the confidence band for the correlation curve corresponding to the upper/lower limits of the 95% CIs on the X axis.

(A) and (B): Estimated correlation curve between clinical efficacy and the virological effect on Day 3 and Day 5, respectively, by the meta-analysis provided by Elias and colleagues (Elias et al, 2024).

(B): Estimated correlation curve between clinical efficacy and virological effect on Day 5 by the meta-analysis provided by Elias and colleagues (Elias et al, 2024).

For the ensitrelvir study results, the data were plotted based on the virological effect at the Day 4 visit in both Figures (A) and (B) because Days 3 and 5 were optional visits in the ensitrelvir studies.

Source: Elias et al. 2024

Notably, the cited studies for other products than ensitrelvir were performed prior to the emergence of omicron virus, and largely in unvaccinated populations.

## 5.3.6. Observational data

Observational data were collected during post-marketing use in Japan. In a retrospective study, the data of 167,310 eligible outpatients aged 18 years or older were identified in a large Japanese health insurance (JMDC) claims database from the date of emergency use approval of ensitrelvir in November 2022 to July 2023, during which Omicron was the dominant variant.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

The study included all outpatients aged 18 years or older who had their first diagnosis of COVID-19 (ICD-10 code: U071, U072) on or after 22 November, 2022, when ensitrelvir obtained emergency regulatory approval from the Ministry of Health, Labour and Welfare (MHLW) in Japan, and before 31 July, 2023, and who could be tracked from 6 months before the date of diagnosis (Day 1) to 1 month following that date. Patients with at least one risk factor for severe disease based on COVID-19 guidelines from MHLW were defined as high-risk patients and were included in the primary analysis population of this study.

Risk factors were similar to the evidence-based risk factors identified by the US CDC and included the following: ≥ 65 years of age, hematologic malignancy, chronic pulmonary disease, chronic renal disease, diabetes, hypertensive disease, dyslipidaemia, cardiovascular disease, cerebrovascular disease, obesity with body mass index ≥ 30 kg/m2, immunosuppressive disorders, and human immunodeficiency virus (HIV)/acquired immunodeficiency syndrome (AIDS), among others (CDC, 2024).

These patients were divided into the ensitrelvir group and the no antiviral treatment group based on their use of antiviral drugs on the date of diagnosis.

The analysis population included all patients who met the eligibility criteria and did not meet the exclusion criteria. The comparison groups were the ensitrelvir-treated group (patients who received ensitrelvir on Day 1) and the no antiviral treatment group (patients who did not receive any antiSARS-CoV-2 drugs on Day 1).

The study outcome was all-cause hospitalization (starting on Days 2-28) as the primary endpoint.

Among 167,310 eligible patients, 5,177 (3.1%) were treated with ensitrelvir, and 162,133 (96.9%) received no antiviral treatment.

Patient distribution was balanced by inverse probability of treatment weighting (IPTW) adjustment. Patient distribution was adjusted by means of the IPTW method using the following characteristics that were considered relevant to the severity of COVID-19: age, gender, and medical history (malignant tumor, chronic respiratory disease, diabetes, chronic kidney disease, hypertensive disease, dyslipidaemia, cardiovascular disease, cerebrovascular disease, morbid obesity, immunosuppressive state, and AIDS/HIV).

The risk of all-cause hospitalisation (the primary endpoint) was significantly lower in the ensitrelvir group than in the no-antiviral-treatment group (0.494% of ensitrelvir-treated patients hospitalised versus 0.785% of patients who received no antiviral treatment; risk ratio: 0.629 [95% CI: 0.420, 0.943]; risk difference: -0.291% [95% CI: -0.494, -0.088].

## 5.3.7. Overall discussion and conclusions on clinical efficacy

## 5.3.7.1. Discussion

## Treatment of COVID-19

## SCORPIO-SR

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## Design of SCORPIO-SR

SCORPIO-SR was a Phase 2/3 study consisting of four independent parts (2a, 2b, 2b/3, and 3). The focus of the efficacy assessment is on the confirmatory Phase 3 part, which evaluated the efficacy and safety of ensitrelvir in adults and adolescents with SARS-CoV-2 infection regardless of risk of progression to severe COVID-19.

All study sites were located in Japan (65), South Korea (26), and Vietnam (2). In Japan, 183 participants were randomised to the 375/125 mg ensitrelvir group and 169 to the placebo group. In South Korea, the corresponding numbers were 16 and 11, respectively, and in Vietnam 148 and 163. Approximately 53% of participants in the 375/125 mg ensitrelvir group and 49% of participants in the placebo group were from Japan; 43% and 48%, respectively, were from Vietnam.

The study consisted of an intervention period (Days 1 to 5), a follow-up period (Days 6 to 28), and an exploratory period (Days 29 to 337).

The regimen included a loading dose of ensitrelvir on Day 1 and maintenance doses on Days 2 to 5. Participants were randomly allocated in a 1:1:1 ratio to one of three treatment groups: the 375/125 mg ensitrelvir group, the 750/250 mg ensitrelvir group, or the placebo group.

It was concluded in the Phase 2b part that the effect of the two dose regimens on viral load and clinical symptoms after 5 days of administration was comparable, and both doses were taken forward for further study. See below regarding alpha allocation.

The planned study population included participants with confirmed SARS-CoV-2 infection and mild to moderate COVID-19 symptoms. The population was not focused on individuals at higher risk of developing severe disease. The inclusion and exclusion criteria are considered acceptable.

Symptom evaluations were performed by the participants themselves and recorded in a study diary according to predefined scales. This approach is considered acceptable.

Overall, the study design was considered adequate.

The primary efficacy evaluation was the superiority of ensitrelvir compared with placebo with respect to the time to sustained resolution of five COVID-19 symptoms (stuffy or runny nose, sore throat, cough, feeling hot or feverish, and low energy or tiredness). The analysis was performed in participants with symptomatic, confirmed SARS-CoV-2 infection who were randomised within 72 hours of symptom onset. In general, antiviral treatments are most effective when administered soon after symptom onset. Therefore, randomisation and treatment within 72 hours have a clear rationale.

The secondary endpoints evaluated the antiviral effect of ensitrelvir compared with placebo based on the change from baseline in SARS-CoV-2 viral RNA up to Day 4 and the time to the first negative SARS-CoV-2 viral titre.

## Statistical methods in SCORPIO-SR

The statistical methods used in SCORPIO-SR are appropriate. However, the primary analysis was based on a late protocol amendment, issued only 3 days before unblinding. This amendment included:

- A change in the primary endpoint, so that only 5 symptoms were evaluated instead of 12;
- A change in the main analysis set, so that more than 45% of all randomised participants were excluded (those who were randomised more than 72 hours after onset of COVID-19);
- A change in the primary statistical test (from a stratified log-rank test to a stratified Peto-

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Prentice's generalized Wilcoxon test); and

- A change in the multiple testing strategy so that, instead of testing each ensitrelvir dose against placebo at a significance level of 2.5% (two sided), only the low dose (375/125 mg) was tested against placebo at a significance level of 5% (two sided).

All four of these changes are necessary to observe a statistically significant effect for the low dose (375/125 mg). Two of the changes (primary endpoint and analysis set) are needed to observe a statistically significant effect for the high dose (750/250 mg).

The lateness of the protocol amendment raises concern. However, it was clarified that the amendment was based on the results of the Phase-2b part of the trial, which did not become available until the Phase-3 part had almost been completed (the phase-3 part was launched while the Phase-2b part was ongoing due to the urgency of the COVID-19 pandemic). Thus, the results were considered to be credible despite the late protocol amendment.

The protocol of SCORPIO-SR initially stated that an interim efficacy analysis would be conducted, but this plan was omitted in protocol version 8.0. The applicant confirmed that no interim efficacy analysis was conducted.

The Data Safety Monitoring Board (DSMB) only had access to blinded safety data and not to efficacy data. The applicant confirmed that no Independent Data Monitoring Committee (IDMC) was established, although this was suggested by the protocol. The applicant also confirmed that neither the applicant nor the contract research organisation staff had access to unblinded efficacy data until completing the database lock and unblinding for the final analysis.

## Efficacy data and additional analyses in SCORPIO-SR

Few participants prematurely discontinued the study: 1.8% (11/607) in the 125 mg group, 3.5% (21/606) in the 250 mg group, and 3.3% (20/608) in the placebo group. Thus, there was no concern that missing data had biased the results. The most common reason was withdrawal by the subject.

Demographics and baseline characteristics were generally similar between study groups. Over 90 % of participants in the three study groups had received COVID-19 vaccinations at some time point prior to baseline (safety analysis set). Though the study was exclusively conducted in East Asia the study population may be deemed representative of proposed use.

In the primary analysis of the primary efficacy endpoint, the median time to sustained (≥ 24 hours) resolution of the 5 COVID-19 symptoms was 167.9 hours (95% CI: 145.0, 197.6) in the ensitrelvir group and 192.2 hours (95% CI: 174.5, 238.3) in the placebo group. Thus, the ensitrelvir group showed a 24.3 hour reduction (95% CI: -78.7, 11.7) in the median time to resolution compared to placebo (p=0.0407). This p-value is borderline.

In the primary analysis, participants were censored if they used prohibited concomitant drugs. When these participants were censored at the time of the last evaluation of COVID-19 symptoms, the p-value increased from 0.0407 to 0.0492, which is borderline significant. This means that the efficacy results of SCORPIO-SR are uncertain.

The primary analysis was supposed to include all participants who had been randomised within 72 hours of the onset of COVID-19 and who had an RT-PCR confirmed SARS-CoV-2 infection at baseline. However, a number of participants (11 in the 125 mg group, 11 in the 250 mg group, and 22 in the placebo group) were excluded because they did not have any of the 5 target symptoms at baseline or because their data on these symptoms were missing. These exclusions are reasonable because they are based on baseline data, so they should not have biased the results.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

There was no nominally significant effect on the primary endpoint in the full ITT population (difference in medians: -10.6 hours; p-value=0.4352). In contrast to the primary analysis population, the full ITT population included participants who had more than 72 hours between treatment and onset of COVID19 symptoms.

Similarly, there was no significant effect when the analysis of the primary endpoint was based on the stratified log-rank test (p=0.1471), which was the primary statistical test before protocol version 10.0, which was issued after the last patient's last visit but before unblinding.

Few participants were hospitalised and there were no deaths during the Phase 3 part. On Day 2 one participant in the 125 mg group was hospitalised without oxygen therapy. During the follow-up period 3 additional participants in the 250 mg group were hospitalised without oxygen therapy.

The CHMP considered that the 5 COVID-19 symptoms (stuffy or runny nose, sore throat, cough, feeling hot or feverish, and low energy or tiredness) were selected as a primary endpoint for the Phase 3 Part as these symptoms were considered characteristic of Omicron strains, which were dominant during Phase 3 Part.

The original primary endpoint of SCORPIO-SR was time to resolution of 12 COVID-19 symptoms. In the exploratory analysis of this endpoint in the ITT, regardless of time from COVID-19 onset to randomisation, no difference was observed between ensitrelvir and placebo. Similarly, no difference between the study groups was observed in the time to resolution of 12 COVID-19 symptoms in the ITT population &lt;72 h from COVID-19 onset to randomisation.

## SCORPIO-HR

## Design and conduct of SCORPIO-HR

This was a global Phase 3 study assessing the efficacy and safety of ensitrelvir in the treatment of participants with symptomatic mild/moderate SARS-CoV-2 infections. Participants with high or standard-risk of severe COVID-19 were included.

The study consisted of two arms, the 375/125 mg dose (the same as in the low-dose arm of SCORPIOSR) and placebo. The participants had a study entry/randomisation visit (Day 1) and study visits on Days 4, 8, 16, and 29, and Weeks 12 and 24.

The efficacy assessment included symptom evaluations, according to defined scales, performed by the participants themselves and recorded in a diary. This was similar to the assessments in the SCORPIOSR Phase 3 part, except that patients scored symptoms once daily in SCORPIO-HR rather than twice daily as in SCORPIO-SR.

A further difference between the treatment studies is that, in SCORPIO-HR, resolution of all targeted symptoms was defined as the first of 2 consecutive days when all targeted symptoms were evaluated as resolved (≥24 h in SCORPIO-SR). Moreover, the number of COVID-19 symptoms to be resolved was increased from 5 in SCORPIO-SR to 15 in SCORPIO-HR. Since it was difficult to predict which variants would be prevalent during the SCORPIO-HR study, a broader and more comprehensive symptom set was included in the primary endpoint. The number of symptoms and the extended symptom resolution time were also in part influenced by FDA recommendations.

The primary efficacy analysis was the superiority of ensitrelvir compared to placebo in reducing the time to resolution of 15 COVID-19 symptoms (cough, shortness of breath or difficulty breathing, feeling feverish, chills, fatigue, body pain or muscle pain or aches, diarrhoea, nausea, vomiting, headache, sore throat, nasal obstruction or congestion, nasal discharge, loss of taste, loss of smell)

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

and participants being alive and without hospitalisation for any reason by Day 29. This analysis was performed in the mITT population which included all randomised participants who took ≥1 dose of ensitrelvir or placebo and (as in SCORPIO-SR) started intervention within 3 days of symptom onset.

The key secondary endpoint was the change from baseline in viral RNA at Day 4. This endpoint can be considered supportive of the clinical primary efficacy endpoint and is similar to the key secondary endpoint in SCORPIO-SR.

Overall, the study design, population, and assessments were considered acceptable.

## Statistical methods in SCORPIO-HR

Appropriate statistical methods were used in SCORPIO-HR.

A major protocol amendment (protocol version 4.0) was issued while the trial was ongoing. This included a change in the primary endpoint (so that 15 instead of 13 COVID-19 symptoms were evaluated), a change in the main analysis set (which excluded patients randomised &gt;72 hours after symptom onset), and an increase in the target sample size. However, there was no concern that this amendment negatively affected the credibility of the efficacy results because the amendment was issued early in the trial (when only 233 out of 2093 patients had been recruited).

## Efficacy data and additional analyses

Few participants prematurely discontinued the study: 6.0% (62/1042) in the ensitrelvir group and 5.4% (57/1051) in the placebo group. Thus, there was no concern that missing data had biased the results. The most common reasons were loss to follow-up and withdrawal by participant.

The majority of the participants in the mITT had critical/major protocol deviations (72%, 1369/1888). The most common were laboratory assessments and patient reported outcomes. This could be considered as a high number of deviations, and it is not clear to what extent this affected the efficacy evaluations. However, this was not further pursued, as the study was not positive and further investigation of this issue was not anticipated to impact conclusions.

The study population included mild/moderate SARS-CoV-2 infected participants, with approximately 31 % of participants having high-risk factors for developing severe COVID-19, and 69 % were deemed to be at standard risk.

Demographics and baseline characteristics in the mITT were generally similar between study drug groups. The majority 73.6 % (1389/1888) had completed the primary series, with the last COVID-19 vaccine &gt; 3 months prior to enrolment. Overall, the study population is deemed to validly represent the intended target population.

The primary endpoint analysis was not met. The difference in time to sustained resolution of all 15 targeted symptoms, being alive and without hospitalisation by Day 29, between the ensitrelvir and placebo groups was -0.6 days (95% CI: -1.38, 0.19, p-value=0.1397).

The number of participants with hospitalisation due to COVID-19 or death due to any cause through Day 29 was few. In the mITT set there were 3 (0.3 %) participants in the ensitrelvir group and 1 (0.1%) in the placebo group with hospitalisation. No deaths were reported through Day 29.

One of the secondary endpoints was time to sustained resolution of 6 targeted symptoms (which were similar to the 5 symptoms evaluated in the primary endpoint of SCORPIO-SR) and being alive and without hospitalisation for any reason by Day 29. The estimate of the difference in restricted mean symptom durations for the ensitrelvir vs placebo groups in the mITT set was -0.7 days (95% CI:-1.49, 0.05) with a nominal p-value of 0.0671. This endpoint was not type I error controlled.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## Overall evaluation of efficacy in the treatment of mild-moderate covid-19

While the SCORPIO-SR study, conducted exclusively in East Asia, reached borderline statistical significance with respect to the primary endpoint, the large, multiregional SCORPIO-HR study failed to demonstrate the efficacy of ensitrelvir. Therefore, the efficacy of ensitrelvir for the treatment of mild/moderate COVID-19 has not been demonstrated.

## Observational study

The applicant provided observational data collected during post-marketing use in Japan. In a retrospective study, the data of 167,310 eligible outpatients aged 18 years or older were identified in a health insurance (JMDC) claims database from the date of emergency use approval of ensitrelvir in November 2022 to July 2023, during which Omicron was the dominant variant.

The risk of all-cause hospitalisation was significantly lower in the ensitrelvir group than in the noantiviral-treatment group (0.494% of ensitrelvir-treated patients hospitalised versus 0.785% of patients who received no antiviral treatment; risk ratio: 0.629 [95% CI: 0.420, 0.943].

The index date of the diagnosis of COVID-19 and that of prescribing or not prescribing ensitrelvir was the same. This avoids time-related bias, which has been seen in some similar observational studies. However, it remains unclear whether the medical examination of all patients was sufficiently similar between patients to ascertain that criteria for prescribing ensitrelvir were uniformly applied. In this context, it is notable that only a small fraction of the patients in the study received ensitrelvir (3.1%), which may raise concern about selection bias.

Moreover, while the authors attempt to adjust for several known risk factors for developing severe COVID-19, the list is not exhaustive. Notably, socio-economic factors are not accounted for.

In summary, this retrospective observational study does not provide sufficiently reliable evidence of a drug effect to support an inference of efficacy for ensitrelvir in the treatment of COVID-19 caused by omicron variant.

Further to the CHMP assessment and conclusions on clinical efficacy, the applicant withdrew the treatment indication from the marketing authorisation application and pursued only the indication for post-exposure prophylaxis.

## Post exposure prophylaxis of COVID-19

## SCORPIO-PEP

## Design and conduct of SCORPIO-PEP

SCORPIO-PEP was a global Phase 3 comparative study in participants who were household members of symptomatic SARS-CoV-2-infected index patients. Participants were randomly assigned in a 1:1 ratio to receive ensitrelvir or placebo.

The study population comprised adults and adolescents without recent respiratory infections living in a household with the index patient (who could be of any age).

The primary efficacy evaluation was the superiority of ensitrelvir compared to placebo in the prevention of symptomatic SARS-CoV-2 infection up to Day 10 in participants living in the same household as an infected individual. Patients were considered to have experienced an event due to 'infection', 'death', 'hospitalisation' and 'study withdrawal'.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

The analysis was to be performed in the mITT population which included participants who were confirmed (by a central laboratory PCR test) to be SARS-CoV-2 negative at screening. Deaths and hospitalisations for any cause were counted as symptomatic SARS-CoV-2 infections.

The key secondary endpoint analysis was an analysis of the primary endpoint in the ITT population, which includes all participants randomly assigned to study intervention (i.e., regardless of central lab PCR confirmation).

Overall, the study design was considered adequate.

## Efficacy data and additional analyses

The SCORPIO-PEP trial was well-conducted from a statistical point of view. Moreover, few participants prematurely discontinued the study: 1.8% (22/1194) in the ensitrelvir group and 2.9% (35/1193) in the placebo group. Thus, there was no concern that missing data had biased the results. The most common reasons were withdrawal by participant and loss to follow-up.

Demographics and baseline characteristics were generally similar between the study groups. Positive serology at screening (Day 1) was detected in 99% of participants in both the treatment and placebo groups. The COVID-19 vaccination status was also the same in the ensitrelvir and placebo groups with 69.9% of participants having a history of COVID-19 vaccination. The majority of participants were recruited in North America 67.1% (1595/2377), while 25.8% (614/2377) were recruited in Japan (safety analysis set).

The primary endpoint was met. Ensitrelvir demonstrated a statistically significant reduction in the proportion of participants with confirmed symptomatic SARS-CoV-2 infection through Day 10 after exposure to household contacts with COVID-19 when compared with placebo, 2.9% (1.97, 4.13) versus 9.0% (7.31, 10.94) in the ensitrelvir and placebo groups, respectively. The risk ratio was 0.33 (95% CI: 0.22, 0.49) (p &lt; 0.0001).

The proportion of participants with hospitalisation, defined as ≥ 24 hours of acute care for any reason through Day 28, in the mITT analysis set was 2/1030 (0.2%) in the ensitrelvir group and 0% in the placebo group. There were no deaths for any reason through Day 28. No additional hospitalisations or deaths were observed when analysing the full ITT population.

The primary endpoint was analysed post-hoc by COVID-19 vaccination history (yes vs no). In the subgroup of participants with any vaccination history, the ensitrelvir group (23/720 [3.2%]) showed a numerical reduction in the risk for symptomatic COVID-19 through Day 10 compared with the placebo group (79/707 [11.2%]). In participants without vaccination history, little difference in the risk for symptomatic COVID-19 through Day 10 was observed, ensitrelvir (7/310 [2.3%]) versus placebo (12/304 [3.9%]). It seems that the risk for symptomatic COVID-19 actually was numerically higher in the vaccinated population. Reasons for this observation are unclear. Notably, no data on the recency of vaccination are available, nor are data available regarding whether prior vaccines were adapted to strains circulating at the time of the study.

The risk of symptomatic COVID-19 through Day 10 in the subgroup of participants recruited in Japan was 11/266 (4.1%) and 62/270 (23%) in the ensitrelvir and placebo groups respectively. In North America, where the majority of participants were recruited, the risk of symptomatic COVID-19 was 15/692 (2.2%) and 25/683 (3.7%) in the ensitrelvir and placebo groups, respectively. Few participants were recruited in the rest of the world. Thus, no meaningful conclusion can be drawn from these data.

No conclusive explanation has been identified for the regional heterogeneity observed in the measured effects. The CHMP considered that the differences in efficacy could be due to multiple factors and may include variables that were not measured in the study. There were no identified intrinsic factors that may affect regional differences in the efficacy of ensitrelvir. Importantly, although the magnitude of effect differed between regions, the direction of benefit consistently favoured ensitrelvir. Furthermore, subgroup analyses were exploratory in nature and were not powered to demonstrate efficacy within individual regions.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Overall, the SCORPIO-PEP study is statistically robust and positive. The CHMP concluded that the efficacy of PEP has been demonstrated and the indication in post-exposure prophylaxis was considered approvable.

Nevertheless, the observed regional heterogeneity introduces uncertainty regarding the magnitude of benefit that may be achieved across different epidemiological settings. A specific context regarding the value of prophylaxis is a scenario with a susceptible coronavirus more prone than omicron variants to cause pneumonia. In general, the value of use in post-exposure prophylaxis depends on the epidemiological situation and should be guided by the advice of public authorities. Therefore, the following statement has also been added as part of the indication: Zokovea should be used in accordance with official recommendations.

Moreover, considering the mode of action and antiviral activity of ensitrelvir, the CHMP was of the view that a treatment study conducted in the event of a pandemic due to a novel (non-SARS-CoV-2) pneumonic coronavirus deemed susceptible to ensitrelvir, will support the ongoing development of the medicinal product. The design, objectives, and key features of this study are intended to be further discussed at a subsequent Scientific Advice.

## 5.3.7.2. Conclusions on the clinical efficacy

Ensitrelvir has been shown to be an active antiviral agent. As outlined above, the efficacy of ensitrelvir for the treatment of mild or moderate COVID-19 has not been demonstrated, whereas its efficacy for post-exposure prophylaxis (PEP) is considered established.

Further to the CHMP assessment and conclusions on clinical efficacy, the applicant withdrew the treatment indication from the marketing authorisation application and pursued only the indication for post-exposure prophylaxis.

## 5.4. Clinical safety

The safety of ensitrelvir has been evaluated in both non-clinical and clinical studies.

The clinical safety evaluation of ensitrelvir is based on 10 clinical studies, of which three multicentre, randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled studies (2206T1331 (SCORPIO-PEP), 2108T1221 (SCORPIO-SR), and ACTIV-2d/A5407 (SCORPIO-HR)) in patients requiring treatment or post-exposure prophylaxis for COVID-19 are the primary sources of clinical safety data. These data are supported by safety and tolerability data from seven Phase 1 healthy participant studies.

The safety analysis populations presented in the dossier and discussed below included all randomized participants or all enrolled participants (depending on study) who received at least one dose of the study intervention. Doses administered in the Phase 2 and 3 studies ranged from the proposed therapeutic dose of 375/125 mg to a higher (double) dose of 750/250 mg. A maximum of 2000 mg as a single dose was administered to healthy adults in Phase 1 studies.

Three pooled analysis datasets were generated and used to summarize the AE data, clinical laboratory test results, and vital sign data, in an Integrated Summary of Safety:

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

- ISS-1 population: Study 2206T1331 (SCORPIO-PEP), the Phase 3 part of Study 2108T1221 (SCORPIO-SR), and Study ACTIV-2d/A5407 (SCORPIO-HR)
- ISS-2 population: All parts of Study 2108T1221 (SCORPIO-SR), including asymptomatic participants, and Study ACTIV-2d/A5407 (SCORPIO-HR)
- ISS-3 population: Multiple-dose cohorts in Phase 1 Study 2102T1211 (SAD/MAD/DDI/FE study) (Cohorts F, G, H, L, M, N, O, Q, R, S, and T).

The ISS-1 population is the main safety reference for this assessment. Within this pooled analysis, safety data from the individual studies are separately summarised, for interstudy and inter-dose group comparisons.

## 5.4.1. Safety data collection

Across the three pivotal Phase 3 studies, the safety of ensitrelvir was determined based on treatmentemergent safety findings, including adverse events (AEs) and changes in clinical laboratory tests, vital sign measurements (blood pressure, pulse rate, respiratory rate, and temperature), electrocardiography (except in Studies 2206T1331 (SCORPIO-PEP) and ACTIV-2d/A5407 (SCORPIOHR)), and physical examination.

Blood samples were collected for assessment of haematology, blood chemistry, and coagulation, and vital signs were measured at various protocol-specified time points for each study.

Electrocardiography was performed at various pre-specified timepoints in all studies except Studies 2206T1331 (SCORPIO-PEP) and ACTIV 2d/A5407 (SCORPIO-HR), and physical examinations were performed at various pre-specified timepoints in all studies. For vital signs, systolic blood pressure, diastolic blood pressure, pulse rate, and respiratory rate were recorded.

For all Phase 3 studies, AEs were collected for each participant from the signing of informed consent through completion of the study on Day 28-29 (depending on study) or early termination. AEs occurring through Days 28-29 days after receipt of study intervention were considered Treatment Emergent Adverse Events (TEAEs).

For the summary of the PT 'High density lipoprotein decreased', the SOC 'Investigations', and the any AEs, pooled analysis of the studies was not performed, because high-density lipoprotein-cholesterol (HDL-C) data were collected differently in the Phase 3 part of Study 2108T1221 (unblinded collection) and Studies 2206T1331 and ACTIV-2d/A5407 (blinded collection to avoid functional unblinding, with results informed to study personnel only after the study had been unblinded).

## Demographics

## Phase 1 studies (ISS-3 population)

The Phase 1 studies included participants ranging in age from 19 to 79 years. A total of three studies (Studies 2102T1211 [SAD/MAD/DDI/FE], 2130T1215 [DDI], and 2305T1218 [DDI]) included Japanese participants, three studies (Studies 2130T1215 [DDI], 2135T1216 [mass balance], and 2305T1218 [DDI]) included only male participants, and one study (Study 2403T1219 [DDI]) included only female participants.

## Phase 3 studies (ISS-1 population)

In the pooled Phase 3 studies (ISS-1 population), the median (range) age was 40 (12 to 91) years, weight was 70 (29.9 to 177.5) kg, and BMI was 25 (7 to 58). Female and male participants were represented in approximately equal proportions. Hispanic or Latino participants comprised around 40% of the population, and Asian participants around 55%. Black or African American participants comprised just 5% of the safety population.

Around 80% of SARS-CoV-2 diagnoses (where relevant) had been made on the basis of antigen testing, the remainder using nucleic acid detection tests. Prior SARS-CoV-2 vaccination history was positive in around 90% of participants overall, but varied between studies, being lowest at around 60% among household contacts treated in study 2206T1331 (SCORPIO-PEP).

Around one third of participants in the ISS-1 population had high risk factors for severe COVID-19 disease (proportion not reported within study 2108T1221 (SCORPIO-SR), one third within study ACTIV-2d/A5407 (SCORPIO-HR), 38% within study 2206T1331 (SCORPIO-PEP)). These risk factors were defined differently across different studies (pre-specified for stratification at randomisation in Study ACTIV-2d/A5407 (SCORPIO-HR) versus programmatically derived from data collected in the electronic case report form in Study 2206T1331 (SCORPIO-PEP), for details see 3.3. Main studies, above) but covered characteristics potentially predisposing participant to severe clinical disease course, including advanced age, chronic diseases including immunocompromised states, and immunosuppressive medications.

## Duration of exposure and follow up

The rate of completion of full protocol-specified treatment duration was high across all studies, and exceeded 97% across all Phase 3 ensitrelvir study arms.

In the pooled Phase 3 studies (ISS-1 population), the proportion of participants who did not complete study follow-up to Day 28-29 (depending on study) was low, ranging from 1.4% to 3.5% across the different study arms.

Important protocol deviations were experienced by around 13% of participants in study 2108T1221 (SCORPIO-SR), with those potentially relevant to safety assessment occurring infrequently (lab data, 2%; overdose/misuse &lt;1%). Important protocol deviations were experienced by &lt;1% of participants in study 2206T1331 (SCORPIO-PEP), with those potentially relevant to safety assessment occurring very infrequently.

Meanwhile, a high proportion of participants in Study ACTIV-2d/A5407 (SCORPIO-HR) had critical/major protocol deviations (72%), most commonly laboratory assessments (57%). These deviations affected treatment and placebo arms equally. Around half of participants were affected by laboratory parameter processing errors leading to protocol deviation reporting, and around one in eight by site staff errors (not further specified). Of 10,840 scheduled visits that actually occurred, 1,323/10,840 (12%) of visits had missing laboratory data, which was similar between the two study arms. Of 2,085 total participants, 768/2,085 (37%) had at least one visit with missing laboratory test data, i.e. not all protocol deviations relating to laboratory testing resulted in missing data.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## 5.4.3. Adverse events

## Phase 1 studies (ISS-3 population)

Across the multiple-dose cohorts in Phase 1 Study 2102T1211, pooled in the ISS-3 population for safety analysis, 82.6% receiving ensitrelvir and 35.7% receiving placebo reported a TEAE. The most common TEAE reported in the study that was considered related to ensitrelvir was high density lipoprotein decreased (101 events).

In study 2127T1213 (Hepatic Impairment), 2 participants (22.2%) in Group A [mild impairment], 1 participant (12.5%) in Group B [moderate impairment], and 1 participant (12.5%) in Group C [normal function] reported TEAEs, with no differences between treatment arms.

In study 2128T1214 (Renal Impairment), 3 participants (37.5%) in Group A (mild renal impairment) and 2 participants (25.0%) in Group B (moderate renal impairment) reported TEAEs. No participants in Group C (severe renal impairment) or Group D (normal renal function) experienced TEAEs.

TEAEs were reported infrequently in the DDI and mass-balance studies (data not shown here) and PTs were generally consistent with those reported across the rest of the clinical programme.

## Phase 3 studies (ISS-1 population)

An overview and summary of TEAEs from pooled participants in the Phase 3 studies (ISS-1 population) are presented in Table 79 and Table 80 below.

Overall, in the pooled Phase 3 studies (ISS-1 population), the most commonly reported TEAEs (≥ 2%) in the pooled ensitrelvir group were blood triglycerides increased, hypertriglyceridemia, creatinine renal clearance decreased, headache, blood bilirubin increased, hyperglycaemia, and low-density lipoprotein increased. The frequencies of these commonly reported TEAEs were consistent also when presented for all parts of study 2108T1221 (SCORPIO-SR) (ISS-2 population, not shown here).

The majority of TEAEs with a reporting frequency of ≥2% across the Phase 3 studies occurred on Days 6 to 7 in participants in all intervention groups. This pattern was most pronounced for study 2206T1331 (SCORPIO-PEP) and all parts of study 2108T1221 (SCORPIO-SR) (ISS-2 population). Meanwhile, in study ACTIV 2d/A5407 (SCORPIO-HR), a notable proportion of TEAEs with a reporting frequency of ≥2% were reported later than this (around 18% of TEAEs observed between Days 8 to 14 and a further 19% observed only on Day 15 or later).

TEAEs were generally mild to moderate in severity. Severe TEAEs were reported at a higher frequency in study ACTIV 2d/A5407 (SCORPIOHR), amongst 88 (8,5%) of participants on ensitrelvir and 109 (10.4%) on placebo, and much more rarely (&lt;1.7% participants in any group) across the other Phase 3 studies, and across all other parts of study 2108T1221 (SCORPIO-SR) (ISS-2 population). Severe TEAEs reported in the pooled ensitrelvir group were creatinine renal clearance decreased in 22 participants, hyperglycaemia in 19 participants, low density lipoprotein increased in 11 participants, lymphopenia and blood triglycerides increased in 10 participants each, hypertriglyceridemia in 7 participants, anaemia and blood glucose increased in 5 participants each, hypercholesterolemia and headache in 3 participants each, alanine aminotransferase increased and aspartate aminotransferase increased in 2 participants each, and nausea and fibrin D dimer increased in 1 participant each.

The incidence of TEAEs that were not resolved at the time of data cut-off was highest in study ACTIV 2d/A5407 (SCORPIO-HR) (9.7% in the ensitrelvir group and 10.5% in the placebo group), lower across all parts of study 2108T1221 (SCORPIO-SR) (8.6% in the 125-mg ensitrelvir group, 8.7% in the 250mg ensitrelvir group, and 5.3% in the placebo group), lower still in the Phase 3 part of study 2108T1221 (SCORPIO-SR) (6.3% in the 125-mg ensitrelvir group, 6.8% in the 250-mg ensitrelvir group, and 3.1% in the placebo group), and lowest in study 2206T1331 (SCORPIO-PEP) (0.5% in both the ensitrelvir and placebo groups). Decreases in HDL and total cholesterol levels observed were transient in the vast majority of cases. These decreases were particularly obvious in the (initially healthy) participants of study SCORPIO-PEP and could have been clinically relevant if of longer-term duration. However, even in those participants where the decreased HDL and total cholesterol levels did not return to baseline by the end of the observation period at Day 28, there were usually increases from the nadir level. Moreover, the levels at the end of the observation period were usually above the lower normal range levels of 40 mg/dL for HDL cholesterol in men and 50 mg/for women and about 125 mg/dL for total cholesterol. In those cases where the levels at the end of the observation period were not above the lower normal range level, the participants had in the vast majority of cases already levels below the lower normal range level already at baseline.

## 5.4.3.1. Adverse drug reactions

Safety data were evaluated from each part of the SCORPIO-SR study once they became available.

When data from the Phase 2a part became available, TEAEs reported in two or more participants in the ensitrelvir group were extracted and evaluated. As the Phase 2a part included a limited number of participants, even a single TEAE resulted in a relatively high incidence rate. Therefore, defining extraction based on incidence rates between the ensitrelvir and placebo groups was considered inappropriate and extraction was based on the criteria described above.

When data from the Phase 2b part became available, among the treatment-emergent adverse events (TEAEs) observed in the Phase 2a and Phase 2b parts, those reported in three or more participants in the ensitrelvir group were extracted and evaluated.

Events showing a temporal relationship to treatment initiation and/or previously described as ADRs for drugs of the same class were also considered ADRs.

When data from the Phase 3 part became available, TEAEs reported in three or more participants in the ensitrelvir group across the Phase 2a, Phase 2b and Phase 3 parts and meeting an additional criterion of a Bayes risk ratio of ≥ 2 were extracted and evaluated.

After completion of the Phase 2a, Phase 2b, and Phase 3 parts of the SCORPIO-SR study and establishment of ADRs based on the above evaluations, the same methodology was applied to the SCORPIO-HR and SCORPIO-PEP studies to identify any new ADRs.

Data were pooled from the ensitrelvir 125mg arms from all three studies to calculate the ADR frequency category.

Anaphylaxis and anaphylactic shock are included as ADRs on the basis of post-marketing reports (see 5.4.10. Post-marketing experience, below).

The following ADRs were proposed for ensitrelvir, with frequency categories:

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## 5.4.4. Adverse events of special interest, serious adverse events and deaths, other significant events

## AESIs

No AESIs were defined in the Phase 1 studies.

Rash was defined (according to the FDA Medical Queries narrow category) as an adverse event of special interest (AESI) in Study ACTIV-2d/A5407 (SCORPIO-HR), therefore 'rash' was analysed post hoc as an AESI in all pooled safety populations. For the pooled Phase 3 studies, there was no notable difference in the incidence of rash between the pooled ensitrelvir and the placebo group (0.8% [27/3430 participants] and 0.6% [18/2840 participants], respectively). None of the AESIs of rash were serious or led to discontinuation of study intervention.

For the AE groups of 'Hypersensitivity' and 'Anaphylactic reaction' a similar analysis was performed, because Hypersensitivity including anaphylaxis is considered a significant adverse reaction with unknown frequency for the direct-acting SARS-CoV-2 antivirals nirmatrelvir/ritonavir and molnupiravir and from post-marketing data for ensitrelvir. Overall, in the pooled Phase 3 studies (ISS-1 population), there was a modest numerical difference in the incidence of hypersensitivity between the pooled ensitrelvir and placebo groups (1.3% [46/3430 participants] and 0.9% [26/2840 participants], respectively). None of these events of hypersensitivity were serious or led to discontinuation of the study intervention. No TEAEs corresponding to the SMQ of anaphylactic reaction were reported in participants in any of the intervention groups in the Phase 3 studies.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## Serious adverse events

There were no serious TEAEs across the Phase 1 studies.

Across the Phase 3 studies, nonfatal serious TEAEs were observed in 8 (0.23%) and 8 (0.28%) participants (22 events) in the pooled ensitrelvir and the placebo groups, respectively; none of these were considered by the investigator to be treatment related. This pattern was consistent within each of the Phase 3 studies/populations.

## Deaths

Across all clinical studies, no deaths were reported in any participant receiving ensitrelvir.

## 5.4.5. Discontinuation due to adverse events

## Phase 1 studies

In part 2 of study 2102T1211, one event each of moderate rash, mild headache, and mild feeling hot was reported as a TEAE leading to discontinuation of study intervention, all of which occurred in one participant in the ensitrelvir 250 mg + midazolam group of Cohort G. These events were considered related to ensitrelvir and not related to midazolam, because of their temporal relationship to the start of the study treatment.

In study 2305T1218, TEAEs leading to discontinuation of the study intervention included nasopharyngitis, erythema, pruritus, and increased CRP.

## Phase 3 studies

Across the Phase 3 studies, TEAEs leading to discontinuation of the study intervention were observed in 17 (0.5%) and 8 (0.28%) participants in the ensitrelvir and the placebo groups, respectively. There was some inter-study variation, with the highest rate of TEAEs leading to discontinuation of study intervention (1%) observed amongst participants in the Phase 3 Part of Study 2108T1221 (SCORPIOSR) who received the high dose 750/250 mg ensitrelvir, and the lowest rate (0.1%) amongst noninfected household contacts in study 2206T1331 (SCORPIO-PEP).

- In study 2206T1331 (SCORPIO-PEP) a TEAE of drug eruption was considered related to study intervention and led to discontinuation.
- In all parts of study 2108T1221 (SCORPIO-SR), events leading to study intervention discontinuation included mild eczema, moderate nausea, mild headache, mild or moderate vomiting, mild or moderate rash, moderate hypertension, moderate drug eruption, moderate abdominal pain, moderate presyncope, mild muscle weakness, and occurred in both the 375/125 and 750/250 ensitrelvir groups.
- In study ACTIV-2d/A5407 (SCORPIO-HR), events leading to study intervention discontinuation included hepatic enzyme increased, nausea, pyrexia, chronic kidney disease and pruritus.
