## Age

Subgroup analyses were performed for the ISS-1 population by age (≥ 12 to &lt; 18 years, ≥ 18 to &lt; 65 years, ≥ 65 years, and ≥ 75 years). For the pooled Phase 3 studies (ISS-1 population), no notable differences were observed between age groups in the percentage of participants reporting treatmentrelated TEAEs in the ensitrelvir or placebo groups (data not shown here).

Study 2108T1221 (SCORPIO-SR) excluded participants &gt;70 years of age, while studies ACTIV2d/A5407 (SCORPIO-HR) and 2206T1331 (SCORPIO-PEP) did not have an upper age limit exclusion criterion. In total, 175 older participants (≥65 years of age) were included in the safety analysis set, comprising 52 patients treated with ensitrelvir in the study ACTIV-2d/A5407, 9 in the Phase 3 Part of Study 2108T1221 and 114 in study 2206T1331. Of these older participants, 5 were treated with the higher (double) dose.

Studies 2108T1221 (SCORPIO-SR) and 2206T1331 (SCORPIO-PEP) included patients from 12 years of age onwards (weighing at least 40 kg in study 2108T1221 (SCORPIO-SR)), who were administered the same dose as adults. Study ACTIV-2d/A5407 (SCORPIO-HR) did not recruit participants &lt;18 years of age. In total, 88 paediatric participants (age group 12 to &lt; 18 years) were included in the safety analysis set, comprising 16 adolescent patients treated with ensitrelvir in the Phase 3 Part of Study 2108T1221 and 72 in study 2206T1331. Of these paediatric patients, 12 were treated with the higher (double) dose.

Sex, race, COVID-19 risk factors, hepatic or renal impairment In addition, sex, race, high-risk factors of COVID-19 (categorised simply as Yes/No, risk factors defined differently in each study), and hepatic (only patients with mild hepatic impairment were included) or renal impairment (only patients with mild to moderate renal impairment were included) had no apparent effect on the safety of ensitrelvir across all Phase 3 studies (data not shown here). This is supported by results of earlier-phase clinical pharmacology studies which indicated no clinically meaningful effects of these factors on PK.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## Pregnancy and lactation

Clinical studies excluded participants who were pregnant, possibly pregnant, lactating, or wished to become pregnant during any of the study periods; therefore, safety in these patients has not been established. There are no data on the transfer of ensitrelvir into human milk. One case was reported of pregnancy while receiving ensitrelvir 125 mg as study intervention in the Phase 2b/3 part of Study 2108T1221 (SCORPIO-SR). No AEs were reported in relation to birth of the on Day 258 (last day in study).

Preclinical data indicate that ensitrelvir and its metabolites cross the placenta and are found in milk in rats. Furthermore, abnormal axial skeleton morphology, such as foetal vertebrae, ribs, and sternebrae, as well as short tails, was observed in rabbits at a dose associated with maternal toxicity, with a low clinical exposure margin to the NOAEL (2.4-fold). In pre- and postnatal development and maternal function studies in rats, administration of ensitrelvir fumaric acid during late pregnancy and lactation affected pup growth and development at doses associated with maternal toxicity. On this basis, administration of ensitrelvir fumaric acid is contraindicated during pregnancy. Women of childbearing potential should avoid becoming pregnant during treatment and for 2 weeks after the last dose (reflecting the approximate time taken for systemic exposures to reach 1% of Cmax due to the long terminal half-life of the product). For lactating women, breastfeeding is not recommended during treatment with ensitrelvir and for 2 weeks after the last dose.

## 5.4.7. Vital signs and laboratory findings

## Phase 1 studies (ISS-3 population)

No clinically relevant trends or changes of clinical concern in clinical laboratory results or vital signs were noted with ensitrelvir administration in the Phase 1 studies, except for transient changes in lipid parameters, including decrease in HDL-C and increase in triglycerides, in a dose-dependent manner.

Effects of ensitrelvir on QTc intervals were assessed using concentration-QTc analysis based on the plasma concentration and ECG data of Japanese healthy adults who received a single oral administration of ensitrelvir (suspension) at doses of 20 to 2000 mg in the fasted state in Study 2102T1211 (SCORPIO-SR). There were no clinically relevant findings in ECG examinations in other Phase 1 studies.

## Phase 3 studies (ISS-1 population)

Analysis of laboratory parameters was performed in the Phase 3 studies (ISS-1) population. TEAEs reported in ≥ 2% of participants in any of the treatment groups that were associated with laboratory tests (SOCs of Investigations and Metabolism and nutrition disorders) were: blood triglycerides increased, hypertriglyceridemia, creatinine renal clearance decreased, blood bilirubin increased, hyperglycaemia, low-density lipoprotein increased, and alanine aminotransferase increased. Most of these TEAEs resolved by the time of follow-up completion (28-29 days), and the severity was mild or moderate.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Summary statistics for clinical laboratory test results at baseline, at the last observation, and for the change from baseline at the last observation in SI units for all intervention groups in the pooled Phase 3 studies (ISS-1 population) were presented in the dossier (not shown here) for standard haematology, liver enzyme, creatinine and electrolyte and blood lipid parameters.

Visual inspection of the data revealed no clinically relevant changes from baseline in any parameter. Minimal changes observed included decreases in HDL-C, increases in triglycerides, increases in total bilirubin and direct bilirubin, increases in serum iron, decreases in unsaturated iron binding capacity, creatinine renal clearance decreased, low density lipoprotein increased, alanine aminotransferase increased, aspartate aminotransferase increased, blood glucose increased and fibrin D dimer increased. These changes were all temporary and returned to baseline or comparable to those observed in the placebo group within 14 days.

Analysis of vital signs was performed in the ISS-1 population (systolic blood pressure, diastolic blood pressure, pulse rate, respiratory rate). Summary statistics for vital signs at baseline, the last observation, and change from baseline at the last observation in SI units for all intervention groups in the pooled Phase 3 studies (ISS-1 population) were presented in the dossier (not shown here). No clinically relevant trends or changes of clinical concern in vital signs were noted with ensitrelvir administration in the pooled Phase 3 studies (ISS-1 population).

There were no clinically relevant findings in ECG examinations in the Phase 3 part of Study 2108T1221 (SCORPIO-SR). ECGs were not performed for Studies 2206T1331 (SCORPIO-PEP) and ACTIV 2d/A5407 (SCORPIO-HR).

## 5.4.8. Post-marketing experience

Ensitrelvir has been approved in Japan since November 2022 for the treatment of SARS-CoV-2 infection under the Emergency Regulatory Approval System. As of 2 March 2025, nearly 2.035 million patients in Japan had been treated with ensitrelvir.

As of 2 Mar 2025, 191 serious adverse reactions in 144 patients had been reported in post-marketing setting. Of these, five or more serious adverse reactions were anaphylactic reaction (17 events), vomiting (8 events), diarrhoea (8 events), anaphylactic shock (7 events), nausea (6 events), delirium (5 events) loss of consciousness (5 events), and melaena (6 events). In addition, 4706 nonserious adverse reactions in 3607 patients had been reported.

A total of 54 cases of maternal exposure during pregnancy have been reported to date from the postmarketing setting. Abortion, spontaneous abortion, and induced abortion were reported in 3, 2, and 4 cases, respectively. Childbirth was reported in 20 cases. Among the childbirths, premature delivery was reported in 2 cases, and ventricular septal defect, gestational hypertension, low birth weight, and transient tachypnoea were reported in one newborn each.

As of 31 March 2026, a total of 26 valid post-marketing cases corresponding to the PTs anaphylactic reaction and anaphylactic shock have been reported. Although detailed information on the temporal relationship was not available for some cases, in most cases, symptom onset occurred within several minutes to several hours after administration of ensitrelvir, indicating a temporal association. With one exception in which the reporter's causality was unknown, all cases were assessed by the reporters as related to ensitrelvir and few alternative causes were suspected by the reporters. On this basis, these PTs are classified as ADRs in the SmPC sections 4.4 and 4.8.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## 5.4.9. Overall discussion and conclusions on clinical safety

## 6.4.7.1 Discussion

## Safety database

Three multicentre, randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled studies (2206T1331 (SCORPIO-PEP), ACTIV-2d/A5407 (SCORPIO-HR) and 2108T1221 (SCORPIO-SR)) provide the primary sources of clinical safety data. The pooling of Phase 3 safety data for this assessment is acceptable, as the study populations of patients and household contacts, respectively, are sufficiently similar and are both adequately represented in the pooled safety database.

In total, around 2000 non-hospitalised patients with asymptomatic to moderate COVID-19 symptoms and over 1000 household contacts of an infected person were exposed to the full proposed treatment dose and duration of ensitrelvir in Phase 2 and 3 studies. Additionally, data from around 1000 patients from Phase 2/3 exposed to a higher-than-proposed (double) dose for up to 5 days provide further safety reassurances. These data are supported by safety and tolerability data from 7 Phase 1, openlabel, healthy participant studies (pooled to provide ISS-3 population).

The clinical development followed a standard approach to collection of clinical safety data. Safety assessments for the Phase 3 studies, and the follow up period of 28-29 days, were proportionate and acceptable for the safety characterisation of a new direct acting antiviral with non-human target intended to be administered for a limited duration of 5 days, considering the safety and tolerability observed in pre-clinical studies and in Phase 1 studies of up to 2000 mg single dose ensitrelvir. Safety data up to 24 weeks is not required for initial authorisation but should be presented for regulatory assessment in due course as the relevant studies (2108T1221 (SCORPIO-SR) and ACTIV-2d/A5407 (SCORPIO-HR)) complete follow up.

In the pooled Phase 3 studies (ISS-1 population), the median (range) age was 40 (12 to 91) years, weight was 70 (29.9 to 177.5) kg, BMI was 25 (7 to 58). Female and male participants were represented in approximately equal proportion.

Around 80% of SARS-CoV-2 diagnoses (where relevant) had been made on the basis of antigen test, the remainder made using nucleic acid detection test. Prior SARS-CoV-2 vaccination history varied between studies, being lowest at around 60% amongst household contacts treated in study 2206T1331 (SCORPIO-PEP). Around one third of participants in the ISS-1 population had high risk factors for severe COVID-19 disease. Background risk factors are themselves not expected to affect the clinical safety profile of ensitrelvir.

The vast majority (&gt;96%) of Phase 3 participants receiving ensitrelvir completed the planned treatment duration and the planned study follow up.

## TEAEs

Across the multiple-dose cohorts in the Phase 1 ISS-3 population, 82.6% receiving ensitrelvir and 35.7% of participants receiving placebo reported a TEAE, with the most common TEAE considered related to ensitrelvir being decreased high-density lipoprotein.

Infected patients in study ACTIV-2d/A5407 (SCORPIO-HR) reported the most TEAEs (around 60% of participants reporting at least one), while non-infected household contacts in study 2206T1331 (SCORPIO-PEP) reported the fewest (around 15%). The most commonly reported TEAEs (≥2%) in the pooled ensitrelvir group were increased blood triglycerides, hypertriglyceridemia, decreased creatinine renal clearance, headache, increased blood bilirubin, hyperglycaemia, and increased low-density lipoprotein. TEAEs leading to discontinuation of study intervention or study participation occurred at a very low rate (≤0.5%) in the Phase 3 clinical programme for ensitrelvir and raise no particular safety concerns.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

TEAEs were generally mild to moderate in severity, and the vast majority (around 90%) had resolved by study completion. Severe TEAEs were reported at high frequency in study ACTIV 2d/A5407 (SCORPIO-HR) (around 10%, but balanced between ensitrelvir and placebo arms) and much more rarely (&lt;1.7%) across the other Phase 3 studies and across all other parts of study 2108T1221 (SCORPIO-SR). Non-fatal serious TEAEs were observed very rarely, in no patient in Phase 2 and in 8 (0,23%) and 8 (0,28%) participants (22 events) in the pooled Phase 3 ensitrelvir and the placebo groups. No deaths were reported in any participant receiving ensitrelvir.

Numerical differences between standard dose, high dose and placebo arms observed in the Phase 3 Part of Study 2108T1221 (SCORPIO-SR), in which these results were shared with the investigators in an unblinded manner, seem to be largely driven by the higher reporting rate of HDL decreased (a known effect of ensitrelvir) observed in the ensitrelvir arms and are thus essentially an artefact of differing study design rather than a true safety finding. No clinically relevant overall trends or changes of clinical concern in clinical laboratory results or vital signs were noted with ensitrelvir administration across the clinical programme.

The reporting rates for events of rash and hypersensitivity (followed as an AESI) across the clinical programme were very low, with only modest numerical differences between the pooled ensitrelvir and placebo arms. None of these hypersensitivity events were serious or led to discontinuation of the study intervention, and there were no reports of anaphylaxis.

## Safety in subgroups

Subgroup analyses were performed for the ISS-1 population (data not shown here) by age (≥ 12 to &lt; 18 years, ≥ 18 to &lt; 65 years, ≥ 65 years, and ≥ 75 years). The pooled Phase 3 studies (ISS-1 population) included 175 older participants (≥65 years) and 88 paediatric participants (12 to &lt; 18 years). In addition, sex, race, high-risk factors of COVID-19 (categorised simply as Yes/No, with risk factors defined differently in each study), and hepatic (only patients with mild hepatic impairment were included) or renal impairment (only patients with mild to moderate renal impairment were included) had no apparent effect on the safety of ensitrelvir across all Phase 3 studies. This was supported by results of earlier-phase clinical pharmacology studies suggesting no clinically meaningful differences in PK. No clinical studies have been conducted in patients with severe hepatic impairment and therefore, ensitrelvir is not recommended for use in this population. No clinically meaningful differences were observed in PK or clinical safety in participants with mild or moderate hepatic impairment compared with participants with normal hepatic function. PK studies in participants with mild to moderate hepatic impairment (Child-Pugh Class A and B) indicate that hepatic impairment tends to decrease ensitrelvir exposure and therefore, does not raise safety concerns. No dose adjustment is necessary in patients

with mild or moderate hepatic impairment.

## Safety in pregnancy and lactation

Clinical studies excluded participants who were pregnant, possibly pregnant, lactating, or wished to become pregnant during any of the study periods; therefore, safety in these patients has not been established. One case was reported of pregnancy while receiving ensitrelvir 125 mg as study intervention in the Phase 2b/3 part of Study 2108T1221 (SCORPIO-SR), with no adverse outcomes reported.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Preclinical data indicate that ensitrelvir and its metabolites cross the placenta and are found in milk in rats. Furthermore, abnormal axial skeleton morphology such as foetal vertebrae, ribs, and sternebrae as well as short tails was observed in rabbits at a dose associated with maternal toxicity with a low clinical exposure margin to the NOAEL (2.4-fold). In pre- and postnatal development and maternal function studies in the rat, administration of ensitrelvir fumaric acid during late pregnancy and lactation periods affected the growth and development of pups at doses associated with maternal toxicity.

Pregnant women represent a group in which the risk of severe COVID-19 is higher than in the general population. However, it is agreed that the non-clinical findings represent an unacceptable level of risk given the therapeutic indication and modest clinical benefit. A contraindication during pregnancy is therefore supported, as is the recommendation that women of childbearing potential should avoid becoming pregnant during treatment and for 2 weeks after the last dose. Sections 4.4 and 4.6 state that pregnancy must be excluded before starting treatment. Additionally, section 4.6 states that women of childbearing potential have to use effective contraception during treatment with ensitrelvir and for 2 weeks after the last dose. This is considered a sufficient measure to avoid pregnancy during ensitrelvir administration and for a clinically relevant period thereafter.

## 6.4.7.1.1 Overall assessment of available safety data

The completed clinical programme provides adequate safety data to inform the benefit-risk assessment for ensitrelvir for the indications of treatment (subsequently withdrawn by the applicant) and postexposure prophylaxis for COVID-19. The most common findings were mild to moderate laboratory abnormalities of limited clinical consequence that resolved after completion of treatment.

## 6.4.7.2 Adverse drug reactions (ADRs) in the SmPC

The methodology applied for the identification of ADRs was reasonable. The frequencies proposed for inclusion in the table in SmPC section 4.8 are calculated based on the pooled data from the 125 mg arms (except for decreased high-density lipoprotein, decreased blood cholesterol, and increased blood iron, as the relevant laboratory values were not measured or were withheld from investigators to maintain blinding in some studies). The incidence of TEAEs tended to be lower in the SCORPIO-PEP study and, while this might be understandable on the basis of the differences in study populations for PEP versus treatment, it has the effect of 'diluting' the ADR frequencies observed in the treatment studies. For most PTs, there is no difference in the resulting frequency category when considering the pooled reporting rate versus study-specific reporting rates. However, for some PTs, there is a major frequency category discrepancy. As the treatment indication was subsequently withdrawn and only the PEP indication is considered approvable, the proposed frequency categories can be considered a sufficiently conservative representation of the clinical safety profile observed among participants receiving ensitrelvir for PEP.

Laboratory changes that are subclinical or do not fall outside the established normal range (e.g. increased triglycerides, bilirubin, and iron, and decreased cholesterol) have been reported as AEs but not included in the SmPC as ADRs, which should generally refer to noxious effects and not merely observed changes.

It is noted that concomitant medication use (which may be substantial in some patients receiving ensitrelvir) is not summarised for the post-marketing cases of anaphylaxis and anaphylactic shock identified from the company database, despite being highly relevant to these AEs; thus, the contribution of other medicines to these safety events cannot be assessed. Nevertheless, it is noted that some events were reported within minutes of the administration of ensitrelvir and resulted in the need for hospitalisation and acute treatment. On this basis, it can be agreed that these PTs should be included as ADRs in the SmPC and that an appropriately concise warning and recommendation in SmPC section 4.4 are supportable.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## 6.4.7.3 Conclusions on clinical safety

The completed clinical programme provides adequate safety data to inform the benefit-risk assessment for ensitrelvir for the indications of treatment (subsequently withdrawn by the applicant) and post-exposure prophylaxis of COVID-19. The most common findings were mild to moderate laboratory abnormalities of limited clinical consequence that resolved after completion of treatment. The observed blood lipid and bilirubin changes were usually mild and transient.

The contraindication during pregnancy as well as the special warnings and precautions for use regarding women of childbearing potential, hypersensitivity and hepatic impairment are considered appropriate.

From the clinical safety perspective, Zokovea is approvable.

## 6. Risk management plan

## 6.1. Safety specification

## 6.1.1. Proposed safety specification

The summary of safety concerns proposed by the applicant in the RMP was updated following the assessment as follows:

Table 83: Summary of safety concerns in the RMP

| Summary of safety concerns   | Summary of safety concerns   |
|------------------------------|------------------------------|
| Important identified risks   | None                         |
| Important potential risks    | None                         |
| Missing information          | None                         |

## 6.1.2. Discussion on proposed safety specification

Further to the outcome of the PRAC discussion, safety in pregnancy, which was initially proposed as missing information, was removed from the RMP. Nevertheless, embryo-foetal toxicity will be addressed as an important potential risk in future PSURs. Of note, the PRAC was of the view that given the amount of evidence available (i.e. non-clinical data on reproductive toxicity), it is more appropriate to refer to embryo-foetal toxicity as an 'important potential risk' rather than 'missing information'. As the data available are considered to fully justify a contraindication during pregnancy, any use of the product in pregnant women can be expected to be limited (off-label use); therefore, conducting dedicated post-authorisation studies is not considered feasible. Consequently, the PRAC

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

concluded that the final list of safety specifications in the RMP should remain empty and that monitoring of embryo-foetal toxicity should be ensured by means of PSUR reporting and assessment.

## 6.2. Pharmacovigilance plan

## 6.2.1. Proposed pharmacovigilance plan

Routine pharmacovigilance activities, such as individual review of adverse event reports, periodic reviews of aggregate adverse event reports, weekly review of published literature, review of Eudravigilance database, discussion of any new signals in PSURs, will be undertaken by the applicant.

The applicant did not propose any additional pharmacovigilance activities.

## 6.2.2. Discussion on the pharmacovigilance plan

## 6.2.2.1. Routine pharmacovigilance activities

The CHMP identified no safety concerns for inclusion in the RMP. Considering the contraindication to use during pregnancy and the recommendation not to use the product during breastfeeding and for 2 weeks after the last dose, additional pharmacovigilance activities are not considered feasible.

However, embryo-foetal toxicity will be addressed as an important potential risk, and use during breastfeeding as an area of missing information in future PSURs; accordingly, these cases will be discussed in future PSURs.

Specific follow-up questionnaires to collect safety information of use during pregnancy and breastfeeding have been removed from Annex 4 of the RMP.

## 6.2.2.2. Additional pharmacovigilance activities

Routine pharmacovigilance activities are considered sufficient to identify and characterise the risks of the product.

## 6.3. Plans for post-authorisation efficacy studies

Not applicable, as there are no imposed post-authorisation efficacy studies.

## 6.4. Risk minimisation measures

## 6.4.1. Proposed risk minimisation measures

The routine risk minimization measures for Zokovea in the EU comprise the SmPC, the package leaflet (PL), and the legal status of the product.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## 6.4.2. Discussion on the risk minimisation measures

## 6.4.2.1. Routine risk minimisation measures

Routine risk minimisation activities as proposed by the applicant are considered sufficient to minimise the risks of the product.

## 6.4.2.2. Additional risk minimisation measures

The applicant did not propose any additional risk minimisation measures, which is accepted.

## 6.5. RMP summary and RMP annexes overall conclusion

The RMP Part VI and the RMP Annexes are acceptable.

## 6.6. Overall conclusion on the Risk Management Plan

The CHMP and PRAC consider that the risk management plan version 0.5 is acceptable.

## 7. Pharmacovigilance

## 7.1. Pharmacovigilance system

The CHMP considers that the pharmacovigilance system summary submitted by the applicant fulfils the requirements of Article 8(3) of Directive 2001/83/EC.

## 7.2. Periodic safety update reports (PSURs) submission requirements

The active substance is not included in the EURD list and a new entry will be required. The new list of Union reference dates (EURD list) entry uses the European birth date (EBD) or the international birth date (IBD) to determine the forthcoming Data Lock Points. The requirements for submission of periodic safety update reports for this medicinal product are set out in the Annex II, Section C of the CHMP Opinion. The applicant did request an alignment of the PSUR cycle with the IBD. The IBD is 22 November 2022.

## 8. Product information

## 8.1. Summary of product characteristics (SmPC)

## 8.1.1. SmPC section 4.1 justification

Further to the CHMP assessment and conclusions on clinical efficacy, the Applicant withdrew the treatment indication from the marketing authorisation application and continued to pursue only the indication for post-exposure prophylaxis of COVID-19.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

The post-exposure prophylaxis indication is aligned with the population studied in the clinical trial SCORPIO-PEP.

The CHMP considered that the value of use in post-exposure prophylaxis depends on the epidemiological situation and should be guided by public health body advice. Therefore, the following statement has also been added as part of the indication: Zokovea should be used in accordance with official recommendations.

## 8.1.2. SmPC section 5.1 justification

The CHMP considered that in addition to the main results from study SCORPIO-PEP, also subgroup results per geographical region should be presented to allow for context dependent decisions on the utility of post-exposure prophylaxis with ensitrelvir.

## 8.1.3. SmPC section 4.3 and 4.6 justification

Due to the potential of ensitrelvir for clinically relevant drug-drug interactions, co-administration of ensitrelvir is contraindicated with medicinal products that are highly dependent on CYP3A for clearance and for which increased plasma concentrations may be associated with serious and/or life-threatening reactions. This risk is further supported by the potential persistence of CYP3A inhibition for 1 to 2 weeks after completion of ensitrelvir administration, reflecting the long half-life of ensitrelvir and its time-dependent mechanism of inhibition.

Preclinical data indicate that ensitrelvir and its metabolites cross the placenta and are found in milk in rats. Furthermore, abnormal axial skeleton morphology such as foetal vertebrae, ribs, and sternebrae as well as short tails were observed in rabbits at a dose associated with maternal toxicity with a low clinical exposure margin to the NOAEL (2.4-fold). In pre- and postnatal development and maternal function studies in the rats, administration of ensitrelvir fumaric acid during late pregnancy and lactation periods affected pup growth and development at doses associated with maternal toxicity. On this basis, ensitrelvir is contraindicated during pregnancy (section 4.3) and not recommended during breastfeeding (section 4.6). Sections 4.4 and 4.6 state that pregnancy must be excluded before starting treatment. Additionally, section 4.6 states that women of childbearing potential have to use effective contraception during treatment with ensitrelvir and for 2 weeks after the last dose. This is considered a sufficient measure to avoid pregnancy during ensitrelvir administration and a clinically relevant period after.

## 8.1.4. SmPC section 4.4 and 4.8 justification

The methodology used to select ADRs for inclusion in SmPC 4.8 is reasonable. The frequency categories can be considered a sufficiently conservative representation of the clinical safety profile observed among participants receiving ensitrelvir for PEP.

There were no reports of anaphylaxis in the clinical trial setting. However, on the basis of postmarketing reports for which there is a reasonable possibility of a causal relationship, anaphylaxis and anaphylactic shock are listed as ADRs in SmPC section 4.8, with corresponding warnings and recommendations in section 4.4.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Other warnings in 4.4 appropriately reflect the complex pharmacokinetic interaction potential, lack of data to support use in severe hepatic impairment, and the need for women of childbearing potential to use effective contraception.

## 9. Labelling

## 9.1.1. User consultation

The results show that the package leaflet meets the criteria for readability as set out in the Guideline on the readability of the label and package leaflet of medicinal products for human use.

## 9.2. Additional monitoring

Pursuant to Article 23(1) of Regulation No (EU) 726/2004, Zokovea (ensitrelvir) is included in the additional monitoring list since it contains a new active substance which, on 1 January 2011, was not contained in any medicinal product authorised in the EU.

Therefore, the summary of product characteristics and the package leaflet include a statement that this medicinal product is subject to additional monitoring and will allow quick identification of new safety information. The statement is preceded by an inverted equilateral black triangle.

## 10. Benefit-risk assessment

## 10.1. Therapeutic context

## 10.1.1. Disease or condition, proposed therapeutic indication

Coronavirus disease 2019 (COVID-19) is an infectious disease caused by severe acute respiratory syndrome coronavirus 2 (SARS-CoV-2). Symptoms of COVID-19 are induced by an inflammatory reaction or immune response to viral infection and include fever, cough, stuffy and runny nose, sore throat, muscle ache, diarrhoea, and taste and smell disorders. Several of these symptoms are similar to symptoms of influenza, with additional manifestations frequently reported.

The incubation period of COVID-19 generally ranges from 1 to 14 days, although it may vary according to the circulating viral variant (CDC, 2021). The majority of infections present as mild to moderate in disease severity with recovery generally observed 1 week after onset of symptoms (NIH, 2024). SARSCoV-2 may be highly transmissible from 2 days before symptom onset until 7 to 10 days after onset (MHLW, 2022).

Some patients may experience severe disease and require hospitalization and oxygen treatment. The known risk factors for severe COVID-19 include age (65 years or older), hypertension, obesity, and smoking (CDC, 2023a), and the risk of mortality increases with increasing age. A certain proportion of infected individuals may remain asymptomatic. It is known that various symptoms of COVID-19 may persist or recur for varied durations in some patients even after recovery, a condition typically referred to as 'long COVID'.

The Applicant proposed the following therapeutic indications:

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Zokovea is indicated for:

- the treatment of mild to moderate coronavirus disease 2019 (COVID 19) in adults and adolescents aged 12 years and older and
- post-exposure prophylaxis of COVID-19 in adults and adolescents aged 12 years and older.

Further to the CHMP assessment and conclusions on clinical efficacy, the Applicant withdrew the treatment indication from the marketing authorisation application and continued to pursue only the indication for post-exposure prophylaxis of COVID-19.

## 10.1.2. Available therapies and unmet medical need

Vaccination against SARS-CoV-2 remains the primary public health intervention to prevent severe disease. While individuals who have maintained recent vaccinations are generally protected from severe disease, the risk of symptomatic infection remains. Moreover, the uptake of COVID-19 vaccines has decreased substantially compared to early in the pandemic, leaving many high-risk individuals vulnerable.

There are no approved oral antivirals for the post-exposure prophylaxis of COVID-19 in the European Union. Therefore, an unmet medical need remains for effective SARS-CoV-2 antiviral therapies in the prevention setting.

## 10.2. Main clinical studies

For a detailed description of the main clinical studies supporting this application, please refer to section 5.3.2 of this document.

The primary efficacy and safety evaluation supporting the proposed indication was based on the pivotal SCORPIO-PEP study.

SCORPIO-PEP was a global, randomised, double-blind, placebo-controlled Phase 3 study conducted in household contacts of symptomatic SARS-CoV-2-infected index cases. Participants aged 12 years and older were randomised 1:1 to receive ensitrelvir (n=1194) or placebo (n=1193). Ensitrelvir was administered as a loading dose of 375 mg on Day 1 followed by 125 mg once daily on Days 2 to 5.

The primary efficacy evaluation was the superiority of ensitrelvir compared to placebo in the prevention of symptomatic SARS-CoV-2 infection up to Day 10 in participants living in the same household as an infected individual.

## 10.3. Favourable effects

Ensitrelvir demonstrated a statistically significant reduction in the proportion of participants with confirmed symptomatic SARS-CoV-2 infection (confirmed by central laboratory PCR; mITT population) through Day 10 after exposure to household contacts with COVID-19 when compared with placebo. The proportion was 2.9% versus 9.0% in the ensitrelvir and placebo groups, respectively. The risk ratio was 0.33 (95% CI: 0.22, 0.49; p &lt; 0.0001).

The proportion of participants with hospitalisation, defined as ≥24 hours of acute care for any reason through Day 28, in the mITT analysis set was 2/1030 (0.2%) in the ensitrelvir group and 0% in the placebo group. There were no deaths from any cause through Day 28. No additional hospitalisations or deaths were observed when analysing the full ITT population.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## 10.3.1. Uncertainties and limitations about favourable effects

The risk of symptomatic COVID-19 through Day 10 in the subgroup of participants located in Japan was 11/266 (4.1%) and 62/270 (23%) in the ensitrelvir and placebo groups, respectively. In North America, where the majority of participants were recruited, the risk of symptomatic COVID-19 was 15/692 (2.2%) and 25/683 (3.7%) in the ensitrelvir and placebo groups, respectively.

No conclusive explanation has been identified for the regional heterogeneity observed in the measured effects. The CHMP considered that the differences in efficacy could be due to multiple factors and may include variables that were not measured in the study. There were no identified intrinsic factors that may affect regional differences in the efficacy of ensitrelvir.

Importantly, although the magnitude of effect differed between regions, the direction of benefit consistently favoured ensitrelvir. Furthermore, subgroup analyses were exploratory in nature and were not powered to demonstrate efficacy within individual regions.

## 10.4. Unfavourable effects

Across the clinical programme, around 2000 non-hospitalised patients with asymptomatic to moderate COVID-19 symptoms and over 1000 household contacts of an infected person were exposed to the full proposed treatment dose and duration of ensitrelvir in Phase 2 and 3 studies, providing placebocontrolled clinical safety data. Additionally, data from around 1000 patients from Phase 2/3 exposed to a double dose for up to 5 days, as well as data from 7 Phase 1, open-label, healthy participant studies, provided additional supportive safety information.

Infected patients in study ACTIV-2d/A5407 (SCORPIO-HR) reported the most TEAEs (around 60%) while non-infected household contacts in study 2206T1331 (SCORPIO-PEP) reported the least (around 15%). The most commonly reported TEAEs in the pooled ensitrelvir group were hypertriglyceridemia, headache, diarrhoea and nausea.

TEAEs were generally (&gt;90%) mild to moderate in severity and the vast majority (around 90%) had resolved by study completion. The observed safety profile was consistent across age subgroups included in the clinical studies, including younger (12 to &lt;18 years old) and older (≥65 years old) participants.

TEAEs leading to discontinuation of study intervention or study, and non-fatal serious TEAEs were observed very infrequently (≤0.5%) across the clinical programme and no deaths were reported in any participant receiving ensitrelvir.

The reporting rates for events of rash and hypersensitivity (followed as adverse events of special interest) across the clinical programme were very low (&lt;1%), with only modest numerical differences between pooled ensitrelvir and placebo arms. None of these events of hypersensitivity were serious or led to discontinuation of the study intervention and there was no report of anaphylaxis. However, postmarketing reports of anaphylaxis and anaphylactic shock with close temporal relationship to ensitrelvir administration and categorised as treatment-related by the reporter have been identified, justifying their inclusion as adverse drug reactions in SmPC 4.4 and 4.8.

Preclinical data indicate that ensitrelvir and its metabolites cross the placenta and are excreted into milk in rats. Furthermore, abnormal axial skeleton morphology such as foetal vertebrae, ribs, and sternebrae as well as short tails were observed in rabbits at a dose associated with maternal toxicity with a low clinical exposure margin to the NOAEL (2.4-fold). In pre- and postnatal development and maternal function studies in the rat, administration of ensitrelvir fumaric acid during late pregnancy and lactation periods affected the growth and development of pups at doses associated with maternal toxicity. In view of this finding and in the absence of adequate clinical pregnancy data, ensitrelvir is contraindicated during pregnancy and not recommended during breastfeeding. Furthermore, women of childbearing potential should use effective contraception during ensitrelvir administration and for 2 weeks after the last dose of ensitrelvir.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Moreover, due to the potential of ensitrelvir for clinically relevant drug-drug interactions, coadministration of ensitrelvir is contraindicated with medicinal products that are highly dependent on CYP3A for clearance and for which increased plasma concentrations may be associated with serious and/or life-threatening reactions. This risk is further supported by the potential persistence of CYP3A inhibition for 1 to 2 weeks after completion of ensitrelvir administration, reflecting the long half-life of ensitrelvir and its time-dependent mechanism of inhibition.

## 10.4.1. Uncertainties and limitations about unfavourable effects

The incidence of treatment-emergent adverse events tended to be lower in the SCORPIO-PEP study. While this might be expected on the basis of the differences in study population for post-exposure prophylaxis versus treatment, it may have an effect of diluting the pooled adverse drug reaction frequencies compared with those observed across the treatment studies.

For most reactions, there was no difference in frequency category when considering the pooled reporting rate versus study-specific reporting rates. For some reactions, however, discrepancies between pooled and study-specific frequency categories were observed. As the treatment indication was no longer pursued, the final frequency categories presented in the SmPC can be considered as a conservative representation of the clinical safety profile for patients who receive ensitrelvir for postexposure prophylaxis.

No pharmacokinetic and clinical data are available in patients with severe hepatic impairment. Therefore, ensitrelvir is not recommended for use in patients with severe hepatic impairment, as reflected in section 4.4 of the SmPC.

## 10.6. Benefit-risk assessment and discussion

## 10.6.1. Importance of favourable and unfavourable effects

The efficacy of ensitrelvir for post-exposure prophylaxis was shown with statistical robustness. No intrinsic factors were identified that would explain the regional difference observed, but the CHMP considered reassuring that all regions showed the same direction of benefit. Nevertheless, the observed regional heterogeneity introduces uncertainty regarding the magnitude of benefit that may be achieved across different epidemiological settings. A specific context regarding the value of prophylaxis is a scenario with a susceptible coronavirus more prone than omicron variants to cause pneumonia. In general, the value of use in post-exposure prophylaxis depends on the epidemiological situation and should be guided by public health body advice.

Ensitrelvir has been shown to be an active antiviral agent inhibiting the SARS-CoV-2 main protease with the potential to impact the course of COVID-19. Moreover, it exhibits broad activity against beta-coronaviruses. In this sense, ensitrelvir may represent a potential component of pandemic preparedness strategies in the event of future outbreaks caused by susceptible viruses.

The clinical safety database is sufficient to consider the clinical safety of ensitrelvir well characterised. Administration of the proposed dose and treatment duration was well tolerated and the clinical safety profile of ensitrelvir in the indicated population is favourable, consisting mainly of mild to moderate non-specific complaints and laboratory abnormalities that were transient in nature and of limited clinical consequence, without evidence of clinical impact or organ injury.

With regard to safety in pregnancy, no clinical data are available, but the pre-clinical signals of embryo-foetal toxicity were considered to preclude ensitrelvir use in pregnancy for the intended indication. Therefore, use in pregnancy is contraindicated and pregnancy must be excluded before initiating administration of ensitrelvir. Nevertheless, in the event of unintended pregnancy, monitoring of embryo-foetal toxicity will be ensured by means of PSUR reporting and assessment.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

The CHMP also considered that the clinically relevant interaction potential of ensitrelvir, in particular its CYP3A inhibitory effect, requires contraindication of concomitant use with medicinal products highly dependent on CYP3A for clearance.

## 10.6.2. Balance of benefits and risks

The efficacy of ensitrelvir for post-exposure prophylaxis of COVID-19 has been demonstrated. The safety profile is well characterised, and the risks are manageable through routine pharmacovigilance and risk minimisation activities.

Taking into account the demonstrated efficacy, the generally favourable safety profile, and the manageable nature of the identified risks, the CHMP considered that the benefits of ensitrelvir outweigh its risks in the post-exposure prophylaxis of COVID-19.

## 10.7. Benefit-risk conclusions

## 10.7.1. At Day 210 - CHMP conclusions

Considering all favourable and unfavourable effects described above, the CHMP considered that the benefit-risk balance of Zokovea for the indication of post-exposure prophylaxis of COVID-19 in adults and adolescents aged 12 years and older is positive.
